# CUBI Project State Ledger

**Snapshot date:** 2026-10-25
**Repository:** `consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform`
**State rule:** Only merged-main behavior counts as implemented completion. PR/branch and documentation-only progress is not product completion.

**P0 — Shared Scheduling Core Performance & Adaptive Execution (2026-11-16)**

- **Priority:** P0, highest CUBI Engineering priority; tracked by [Issue #1992](https://github.com/consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform/issues/1992).
- **Canonical assessment:** [P0 Scheduling Core Optimization Feasibility](../engineering/P0_SCHEDULING_CORE_OPTIMIZATION_FEASIBILITY_2026-11-16.md).
- **Baseline reviewed:** `main` `9d496a219dd39250ff220052d62111b6cdcb958c`.
- **Status:** P0-A through P0-I implemented, measured and merged through `648cef68` (PRs #1993–#1998); P0-J phases 1–2 merged as `92a62afd` (PR #2021) and phases 3–5 merged as `b6b3cb71` (PR #2022); P0-K (exact working-day index) merged as `57291794` (PR #2050); P0-L (time-aware capacity index and resolver memo) merged as `02704eea` (PR #2053). One canonical engine is retained; every optimization and the parallel float path passed exact canonical-output differential tests. The only remaining gaps are packaging/runtime gates: no desktop installer supervision test and no proven portable mobile runtime for the Python engine.
- **Merged phases:** P0-A/B seeded baselines + heap topological ordering; P0-C time-aware free-float bounded search; P0-D leveling demand ledger; P0-E exact CPM reuse; P0-F durable PostgreSQL job lifecycle; P0-G adaptive CPU/RAM worker budget + job executor (1.96x at 2 workers, 3.45x at 4 on this 4-core host); P0-H exact parallel per-activity time-aware floats (2.27x at 4 workers, opt-in, identical hashes); P0-I self-contained offline bundle with identical canonical output and a fail-closed network guard. P0-J leveling rebuild removal + verified closed forms (`92a62afd`), then safe parallel float execution and a real PostgreSQL job-path stress matrix (`b6b3cb71`, measured at `87fc5c4a`); P0-K exact working-day index for calendar arithmetic (`57291794`, measured at `2c194851`). P0-L exact time-aware working-day capacity index, registry resolve memo and exact Decimal fast path (`02704eea`, measured at `9909695f`).
- **P0-J measured (2026-11-17, merged `b6b3cb71`; 4 effective CPUs):** date CPM 25k 1,725 ms and time-aware 25k 8,153 ms at 2.1–2.2x vs `ee9753e6`; forward leveling 500 activities 157.0 s → 83 ms (1,888x, identical 476 shifts), backward 100 activities 45.7 s → 17 ms; time-float fork pool 1.5x whole-schedule (float pass 3.3x) with identical canonical hash at 1/2/4 slots, and exact sequential fallback inside the threaded job executor because a 11.6 MB pickled context costs 25 µs/activity of transfer against 169 µs of work. Real job stress: 100/500/1000 users × 1/2/4 workers completed with 0 errors, 0 pending and one canonical hash per case (median 2.652→0.798 s, 9.535→4.088 s, 19.137→8.467 s); 8/16/32/64 processes on 4 CPUs gave 177.4/173.9/144.1/98.4 jobs/s with 82 PostgreSQL connections at 64, so multi-worker claims stop at real capacity.
- **Measured, not extrapolated:** concurrency numbers cover 4 effective cores only; 16/32 workers are refused by the harness. Desktop/mobile packaging, multi-host PostgreSQL, HTTP admission and leveling-bundle execution are not claimed.
- **P0-K measured (2026-11-18, after `20778a03`; 4 effective CPUs):** exact working-day index in `WorkingTimeResolver` replaced day-by-day loops for calendars with a plain weekday set and canonical holidays; exception overlays and degenerate calendars keep the original loops, which are also the differential oracle (`tests/scheduling/test_working_day_index_equivalence.py`, 32 tests). Date CPM 1,000/5,000/25,000/100,000: 61.8→48.3, 317.6→253.3, 1,665.6→1,335.1, 7,427.2→6,058.4 ms (1.23–1.28×); cross-shape 5k: chain 1.21×, wide 1.36×, fan 1.26×, mixed 19.3×, slack 231.6×; time-aware and leveling unchanged (1.00×/1.01×); peak RSS within ±0.9 %; every canonical hash identical. Full suite 4,921 passed / 131 skipped at `2c194851`; merged as `57291794` (byte-identical code) with post-merge 5,008 passed / 132 skipped.
- **P0-L measured (2026-11-18, after `afc8c701`; 4 effective CPUs):** `TimeAwareWorkingTimeResolver` now keeps an exact per-day capacity profile keyed by the effective interval tuple and skips whole days after walking the first partial day; `calculate_working_hours` measures boundary days exactly and keeps the verbatim loop for mixed time zones; `CalendarResolverRegistry.resolve` memoises its immutable validated views; `_duration_microseconds` avoids the Decimal string round-trip for exact ints/Decimals. Time CPM 1,000/5,000/25,000/100,000: 319.6→223.9, 1,598.7→1,134.0, 8,104.5→5,792.7, 33,361.4→23,960.1 ms (1.39–1.43×); mixed calendars 5k/25k 1,763.4→1,284.6 and 9,084.6→6,686.1 ms (1.36–1.37×); phase split at 25k forward 910.8→703.9, backward 2,953.8→2,292.1, float 4,152.1→2,743.6 ms; date/leveling/backward leveling unchanged within noise (0.99–1.00×); every canonical hash identical; peak RSS flat (100k 384,984→385,112 kB). The previous loops remain the oracle (`tests/scheduling/test_time_capacity_index_equivalence.py`, 86 tests) and the mixed-zone fallback; a +1 µs capacity mutation is detected. Full suite 5,272 passed / 1 skipped at `9909695f`, and 5,272 passed / 1 skipped post-merge on `02704eea` (byte-identical tree); the single observed job-path failure reproduces on unmodified `afc8c701` (SQLite harness race).
- **Priority rule:** perform this work before Engineering enhancements that change or depend on Scheduling, Calendar, CPM, Float or Resource Leveling. Preserve unrelated work that does not destabilize the core.
- **Non-subtractive acceptance:** maintain one canonical Engine and exact P6 Professional Version 26 behavior. Every optimization and parallel execution path must pass complete canonical-output differential tests.
- **First scaling direction:** process-based Workers for independent immutable schedule jobs; adaptive CPU/RAM-aware concurrency; then separately assess intra-project parallelism. Resource Leveling candidate decisions remain deterministic and globally coordinated unless exact independence is proven.
- **Verification limitation:** measured on one 4-core host with local PostgreSQL 14.24; CI success alone is not performance proof. No user-capacity claim is approved beyond the recorded measurements on named hardware and exact code SHA.



## Workspace two-rail slice — Issue #1797 — 2026-11-13 (PR pending review)

- **Scope:** owner comment `6867521682`. Left rail icon duplicates and aliases; right rail contextual Add (+) and record-level commands. No header, menu, login, route or shell redesign; no new destinations; the saved table/Gantt and activity/Details ratios are untouched.
- **Branch and commits:** `feat/vercel-preview-fixtures` — `eb0985fb` (rails) then `5b7e5c53` (incremental Add repaint). Open PR **#1923** (head `5b7e5c53`); **not merged**. Prevailing preview: `https://cubi-web-preview-git-feat-vercel-p-8eb184-foroughichamshir-7811.vercel.app`.
- **Left rail:** the EPS and Activities aliases that duplicated Project and Schedule were removed (21 window destinations remain). Project, WBS, Schedule, Progress, Resources, Assignments, Roles, Calendars, Cost, Expenses, Documents, Reports, Tracking, Issues, Risks, Thresholds, Control, Settings, Administration, Import/Export and Backup/Recovery now read one typed `WORKSPACE_WINDOW_ICONS` table shared with the flyout and desktop shell. New original 16×16 stroked glyphs (`assistant`, `backup`, `details`, `exchange`, `expense`, `proposal`) and separate shapes for issues (circle alert), risks (triangle), warning (octagon), tracking (crosshair) and group (panels); the registry test proves every icon path is unique.
- **Right rail:** Add is the fixed first control; its menu is derived per window from the active menu, selected activity, WBS presence, calendar catalog and session role (`planner`/`project_admin`), and every item dispatches an existing `DesktopCommand` (`project.add-activity`, `edit.link`, `project.add-wbs`, `resource.create`, `assignment.create`, `role.create`, `calendar.create`, `document.create`, `risk.create`, `issue.create`, `expense.create`, `threshold.create`, `activity.assign-resources`). Module commands open the owning window and click the module's own New control through `pendingDesktopAction`; no second create flow exists. Missing services stay disabled with the exact reason, viewers never write, and Progress/Tracking/Cost/Reports/Settings/Administration/Exchange/Backup show a truthful "no create action" state. The rail also carries Comments, Notebook, Details, Suggested actions, Assistant and Help; it contains no `data-menu` destination, so it never repeats left navigation.
- **Assistant/flyouts:** `mountGlobalAssistantLauncher()` now returns early on `/app`; the closed `#cp-ai-action-panel` pill is hidden and both assistant panels open clear of the 40 px rail. The rail assistant/proposals buttons are the single entry points, with `aria-expanded` mirrored and focus restored.
- **Tests:** `apps/web` `npm test` **1096 tsx + 14 node tests passed**, `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` produced the served bundle. New coverage: `workspace-context-add.test.ts` (per-window commands, viewer gating, data reasons, unique ids) and uniqueness assertions in `workspace-icons.test.ts`;
  `workspace-view.test.ts` covers Add-first, no-destination repetition, disabled reasons, empty-window state and the incremental repaint.
- **Browser evidence (deployed preview, real Chrome):** `apps/web/e2e/vercel-preview-workspace.mjs` **18/18 checks, 0 errors**, including 21 unique left glyphs, Add-first in LTR and RTL, open menu with real command ids, ArrowDown/Escape/focus restore, Help → shortcuts dialog, Add Activity → the real create form, per-window Add states, single assistant entry, no floating pill, no API 5xx or browser exception. Screenshots and JSON: `docs/workspace/evidence/workspace-rails-v26-2026-11-13/` (1280×720, 1366×768, 1920×1080, fa RTL). Status **PENDING_REVIEW** — implemented and browser-verified on the preview branch, not merged; no Oracle parity claim.
- **Deliberate exclusions:** project creation stays on the session/project-selector surface (there is no in-workspace create-project command), so the Projects window offers Add WBS/Add Activity only after the project is open. No form/attachment command was added where no service exists.

## ACT-003 filter Cut, Clear and read-only guards — Issue #1797 — 2026-11-14 (preview branch; PR #1923)

- **Scope:** owner comment `6867521682`, P3 acceptance list. The Filters manager already had Named filters, All/Any, Parameter/Is/Value/High Value, multiple ordered criteria, typed operators, relative CD/CS/CF dates, live preview, Apply, Delete, Copy, Paste, Duplicate, Make Global, Copy as Layout and layout persistence. This slice adds the two missing actions and removes dead controls: **Cut** (session clipboard + delete for saved global/layout/user filters) and **Clear** (empties the definition editor without applying or deleting anything). Default filters now render Delete, Cut and Modify **disabled with a localized reason**; previously those buttons submitted a no-op.
- **Code commit:** `5fe7cf0210d1a0374c91d293692d8948f9a355da` on `feat/vercel-preview-fixtures`. Files: `apps/web/src/workspace-dialogs.ts`, `apps/web/src/workspace-desktop-shell.ts`, `apps/web/src/workspace-dialogs.test.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/activity-filters-v26.mjs`. No schema, API, route, schedule or layout-contract change.
- **Behaviour:** Cut is Copy plus Delete, so a cut filter leaves its category and Paste recreates it in the chosen category with the same name, criteria, match mode and scope choice; Cut refuses a `builtin:*` filter. Clear resets name, editing id, scope, match and criteria to one blank row and refreshes the live preview; it never writes the layout. Selecting a saved row re-enables Delete/Cut/Modify and clearing the selection disables them again. Criterion reordering (`data-filter-row-move`), Cut/Paste clipboard, Apply, OK and Cancel keep the dialog open/closed exactly as before; no unwanted reopen path was introduced.
- **Unit tests at the code commit:** `apps/web` `npm test` **1099 tsx + 14 node tests passed** (three new cases: Cut→Paste round trip, Cut refuses a default filter, and disabled/enabled state for Delete/Cut/Modify). `npx tsc --noEmit`, `tsc -p tsconfig.test.json --noEmit` and `npm run build` are clean.
- **Browser evidence (local full-stack preview, real Chrome):** `apps/web/e2e/activity-filters-v26.mjs`, contract **`cubi-activity-filters-v26.v3`**, **14/14 scenarios**, zero exceptions, zero console errors, zero API 5xx. `docs/workspace/evidence/activity-filters-v26-2026-11-14/activity-filters-v26.json` records `testedCodeSha`/`gitHead` `5fe7cf02` and 13 screenshots, including `12-filter-cut-paste-en-1366.png` and `13-filter-clear-fa-rtl-1280.png`. The run also proved Cut/Paste survives Apply and a full reload, and that Clear leaves the applied grid unchanged.
- **Deployed preview evidence (real Chrome):** the same suite ran at `https://cubi-web-preview-git-feat-vercel-p-8eb184-foroughichamshir-7811.vercel.app` at the same code SHA — **14/14 scenarios**, zero exceptions, zero API 5xx. `docs/workspace/evidence/activity-filters-v26-2026-11-14-deployed-log/activity-filters-v26.json` records `baseUrl`, `testedCodeSha` `f203c0b9` (docs head; code `5fe7cf02`) and the new `layoutWrites`/`consoleErrorLog` diagnostics. Six recovered `REVISION_CONFLICT`/`IMMUTABLE_LAYOUT_REVISION` console entries and their matching 409 layout POSTs are the preview runtime resetting its ephemeral `/tmp` SQLite between requests: repeated `GET /p6/layouts/project/activity/revisions` returned `[1]` again after saves had reached revision 3, and no scenario failed because the Web client reloads the authoritative head. This is a **BLOCKED_EXTERNAL preview-runtime limitation for cross-request write persistence**, not an application regression; the local full-stack run on a fresh database and the same suite with 400 ms CDP network latency both produce seven sequential `200` layout saves and zero conflicts. A second deployed run that started after a mid-run reset lost the applied filter on recovery and failed scenario 03, which confirms the same external reset rather than a UI defect.
- **Test-suite repairs:** the fixture now exposes 20 activities across two projects (14 in the active project); the old hard-coded `=== 14` check was relaxed to “at least the original 14”. Scenario 10 now explicitly clears the `All Activities` state that scenario 06 intentionally leaves behind, so the suite is deterministic from a fresh database. These are test-only corrections; no product behaviour was changed for them.
- **Remaining gaps (explicit):** no tenant-wide filter sharing (Make Global remains a project scope label), no nested AND/OR groups, Cut/Paste is per browser session and not an OS clipboard, and the filtered list still scrolls inside the dialog at 1366×768 with a long catalogue. Status **PENDING_REVIEW** on PR #1923; not merged.

## ACT-014 Activity Details read states and stale-response protection — Issue #1797 — 2026-11-14 (preview branch; PR #1923)

- **Scope:** P5 acceptance for the Activity Details pane: Idle / Loading / Loaded / Empty / Error / **Unauthorized** states, stale-response protection, no fabricated data. PR #1793's links and the 17-tab inventory are untouched.
- **Code commit:** `5b18346bbf8c999463dd780e21f13fc26b3b4522` on `feat/vercel-preview-fixtures`. Files: `apps/web/src/workspace-activity-details.ts`, `apps/web/src/workspace-activity-details.test.ts`, `apps/web/e2e/activity-details-v26.mjs`. No API, schema, route or schedule change.
- **Behaviour:** `ActivityDetailsLoadStatus` now carries `unauthorized`; `isUnauthorizedDetailsCode()` classifies `AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`, `PROJECT_NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_AUTHORIZED`, `UNAUTHORIZED`, `FORBIDDEN`, `SESSION_*`, `AUTH_*` and any `NOT_AUTHORIZED` code from the real transport result. Every tab renders a localized permission message (`data-details-unauthorized`, `role="alert"`) instead of printing the transport code as a service failure, and never shows an empty table as if the data did not exist; the UDF-values block has its own denied state. Stale protection now invalidates the in-flight token as soon as the `tenant:project:revision:activity` key changes, **including the cached path where no new request starts** — previously a slow read for the previous activity could still pass the token check and paint its rows after a fast switch to a cached activity.
- **Unit tests at the code commit:** `apps/web` `npm test` **1100 tsx + 14 node tests passed** (new case: English and Persian unauthorized rendering, no raw code, classifier truth table). `npm run typecheck`, `tsc -p tsconfig.test.json` and `npm run build` are clean.
- **Browser evidence (local full-stack preview, real Chrome):** `apps/web/e2e/activity-details-v26.mjs`, contract `cubi-activity-details-tabs-v26.v1`, exit 0 with 17 tabs, all seven data tabs settled without error, three real writes read back, Customize Tabs surviving reload, zero exceptions/console errors/API 5xx. Two new probes: a patched 403 on `/cost/expenses` renders “You do not have permission to view these records in this project.” with no `AUTHORIZATION_DENIED` leak and recovers after the probe is removed; a deliberately delayed A130 steps read (`slowHits: 1`) followed by an immediate switch to A120 leaves the Steps panel showing “No steps are stored for this activity.” instead of A130's rows. Evidence `docs/workspace/evidence/activity-details-tabs-v26-2026-11-14/` records `testedCodeSha`/`gitHead` `5b18346b` and screenshots `details-unauthorized-en-1366.png`, `details-stale-response-en-1366.png`, `details-en-1366.png`.
- **Deployed preview evidence (`9518d274` deployment, code `5b18346b`):** the same suite ran against the Vercel alias with `CUBI_PERSISTENCE_MODE=deployed-ephemeral` and `CUBI_EVIDENCE_DATE=2026-11-14-deployed`. All 17 tabs rendered, the 403 probe still produced the localized permission message with no code leak, the delayed A130 read still could not paint into A120 (`slowHits: 1`, `selected: "A120"`, `loading: false`), Customize Tabs survived a full reload, and there were zero exceptions/console errors/API 5xx. Write read-back was **BLOCKED_EXTERNAL**: each save reported success but the following read showed the seed row, because the managed preview serves requests from an ephemeral database snapshot (the same reset documented for ACT-003). The suite now has an explicit `deployed-ephemeral` mode that records `persistenceBlocked` per write instead of silently passing; the strict default still fails on a missing read-back, and the local full-stack run remains the authoritative write evidence. Bundle `docs/workspace/evidence/activity-details-tabs-v26-2026-11-14-deployed/`.
- **Remaining gaps:** WPs & Docs and Update History stay read surfaces; Risks has no activity reference; Notebook defers to the browser-local Comments rail; the pane is still one active tab in the DOM, so only the active tab can show a state at a time; no per-tab unauthorized illustration beyond the text alert. Status **PENDING_REVIEW** on PR #1923; not merged.

- **Containment method:** for every merged branch, the files it changed relative to its merge base were compared byte-for-byte with the corresponding squash merge commit; ten branches matched (`0` differing files) or differed only where main has newer code or evidence. `git cherry` marks every commit `+` because squash merges change patch identity; ancestry and file content, not `cherry`, decided the disposition.
- **Preservation actions:** six tips were absent from origin and were pushed without force as `feat/act-011-gantt-overlays`, `feat/v26-stage-d3c-resource-calendar-assignment`, `feat/v26-stage-d3d-calendar-cycle`, `feat/v26-stage-e-risks-thresholds`, `preserve/preserve-rebased-20261113` and `ws-007-saved-layout-lifecycle`. No PR was opened for them because their changes are already in main; a PR would duplicate merged work. Four other local tips were already present on origin. No branch was deleted.
- **Open stale PRs deliberately untouched:** #1725 (`CONFLICTING`, 84 commits), #1726 (draft, `MERGEABLE` only against the #1725 branch) and #1728 (`CONFLICTING`, 9 commits) remain open for traceability. They are older implementations superseded by merged ACT work and were **not** merged, rebased or bulk-integrated.
- **Uncommitted or untracked work:** none. All generated evidence for the latest slice is committed in `f5825f7f` and is present in main as `6ada3daa`. No recovery bundle or patch was required.
- **Known limitations (explicit, not VERIFIED):** ACT-002 caps grouping at three levels; the catalog is the six fields above; “No value” is always last regardless of direction; collapse state is browser-local (`cubi:workspace-wbs-collapse`), while the level plan is saved in layout metadata; deleting a stored layout is still unsupported by the layout service, so the UI disables Delete with an explanation (`apps/web/src/workspace-layout-selector.ts`, `canDelete: false`). Select-all/count reconciliation/search-driven selection/export scope remain unaudited (ACT-020). No production deployment, external environment or Oracle numeric parity is claimed.
- **Exact next implementation task:** ACT-020 remainder — implement and browser-verify **Select All, row-count reconciliation, full-project search and export scope (all / filtered / selected)** against the workspace read API, using the official Help topics for Find, Select All and Export; code paths `apps/web/src/workspace-model.ts`, `workspace-view.ts`, `main.ts` and the Activities toolbar; derive every expected count from the read model rather than fixture constants, and keep selection in-memory until the export/selection contract is defined.
- **Status vocabulary for this entry:** ACT-002 = WIRED_WRITE (implemented, tested, merged); preserved legacy branches = merged or superseded; stale PRs = open/unmerged; ACT-020 remainder = WIRED_READ → next.

## Agent 3 checkpoint — Issue #1799, independent security review reconciliation and SEC-006 — 2026-11-13 (merged; PR #1880; independent review SATISFIED)

- **Base and merge:** branch `fix/agent3-sec006-retention-shared-bytes` cut from merged `main` `982125a`, head `439d42a`, squash-merged as **`cdc9999`** via PR #1880 after exact-head CI at `439d42a`.
- **Independent review completed, not restarted:** both previously dispatched reviewers returned full registers after the Workstream D checkpoint was written. Reviewer A (commit `d56152e`, full surface, 13 findings) reported one High and no Critical; reviewer B (commit `392326b`, bounded file set, 11 findings) reported no Critical and one High transport defect that `07d3c5e` had already fixed, which it re-tested and confirmed. `docs/audits/BACKEND_SECURITY_REVIEW_2026-11-13.md` now maps both reviewer identifiers to register IDs SEC-001…SEC-025, records each finding with evidence, impact, recommendation and required negative test, and states the reviews were static only. The **independent-review gate is satisfied**; runtime, DAST/fuzzing, penetration-test, production-configuration and unassessed-module gates remain **OPEN**.
- **SEC-006 (High, fixed):** reviewer A found that `DocumentRetentionService.dispose()` deleted the content-addressed object for one `(document_id, version)` without checking live siblings, while object paths are digest-derived per project and reconciliation already protects such objects. Disposing one matured version could therefore destroy bytes still served by another live document. `dispose()` now snapshots project content versions and dispositions and removes the object only when no other non-disposed version shares the `storage_ref`; the disposition is still recorded. An unreadable snapshot raises `DOCUMENT_RETENTION_REFERENCE_CHECK_FAILED` (`500`) and removes nothing (fail closed, retryable, no partial write). Residual race: a concurrent identical-bytes upload committing metadata between snapshot and delete; reconciliation reports that as `metadata_only`, and eliminating it needs a cross-store lease.
- **Negative tests:** `test_dispose_preserves_bytes_shared_with_a_live_sibling` (two documents, identical bytes; sibling stays readable and downloadable, last live reference removes the object), `test_dispose_refuses_to_guess_when_reference_metadata_is_unreadable` (bytes intact, no disposition row), and `test_dispose_keeps_bytes_shared_with_a_live_sibling_live` (PostgreSQL adapters against a live server). The existing single-reference dispose test is unchanged and passes.
- **Open findings carried explicitly:** SEC-007 Medium legal-hold/disposition permission level; SEC-008 Medium XLSX declared-vs-inflated size bound (reported independently by both reviewers); SEC-009 Medium unbounded formula-parser recursion; SEC-010 Medium unscoped/unbounded `list_all` and document list; SEC-011 Medium S3 cleartext-HTTP acceptance and readiness HTTPS gap; SEC-012 Medium client-supplied `storage_ref` bypassing version-scoped disposal; SEC-013 Medium reconciliation treating document-record-only references as orphans; SEC-014 Medium delete completion not bound to the claim token; SEC-015 Medium `GET /api/session` reflecting the session credential; SEC-016…SEC-024 Low (LIKE escaping, reflected exception codes, `error_dto` catch-alls, AI self-approval, knowledge-fetch redirects, metrics auth metering, `CUBI_AI_ENABLED` environment source, anonymous readiness detail, WSGI short reads); SEC-025 Info test-only AI adapter. None is closed by this checkpoint.
- **Verification:** Python 3.12.13, `psycopg` 3.3.9, PostgreSQL 16.2 on the same database for both runs. Focused (`test_document_retention.py`, `test_document_api_v1.py`, `test_document_storage_reconciliation.py`, `test_document_application_reads.py`) → **44 passed**. Full SQLite suite → **4064 passed, 176 skipped**. Full live PostgreSQL suite, twice consecutively → **4295 passed, 1 skipped** both runs. Exact-head CI at `439d42a`: PostgreSQL (`43364970255`), test 3.11/3.12/3.13 (`43364970259`), four typechecks (`43364970303`) — all **success**.
- **Status:** implemented, locally tested, PostgreSQL tested, merged. No consolidated `Critical`/`High` finding is unresolved. Medium/Low findings are open and tracked in severity order. No production configuration, deployment, DAST or penetration test is claimed.

## Agent 3 checkpoint — Issue #1799, operational metrics export — 2026-11-13 (merged; PR #1867)

- **Base and merge:** branch cut from merged `main` `77260c4`, squash-merged as **`8482775`** via PR #1867 after exact-head CI at `d56152e`. Scope is Workstream C.
- **Exporter:** `construction_pm.metrics_export` provides `MetricsExportSettings` (environment-driven, default disabled), `MetricsSource` and `PrometheusTextExporter` protocols, `DeterministicPrometheusExporter` and `MetricsExportService`. States are `disabled`, `configured` and `unavailable`. The exporter depends only on a read-only `snapshot()`; a deployment can substitute its own renderer.
- **Deterministic bound output:** series are ordered by metric name, label block and fixed sample order; counters render as `<name>` and observations as `_count`, `_sum`, `_min`, `_max`, `_mean`. Numbers are formatted without locale variation and never as NaN/Inf. Output is capped by `CONSTRUCTION_PM_METRICS_EXPORT_MAX_BYTES` (default 256 KiB, hard maximum 4 MiB) and an over-cap exposition fails closed with `METRICS_EXPORT_OUTPUT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` instead of returning a partial body.
- **Access and states:** `GET|HEAD /api/metrics` exists only when export is enabled. Disabled → 404; missing/malformed/wrong bearer → constant-time comparison and 401 with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer realm="metrics"` without echoing the token; misconfigured, no registry, oversized or renderer failure → 503 with a static code or exception class name only; other methods → 405. Responses are `text/plain; version=0.0.4`, `Cache-Control: no-store`, and bounded by `Content-Length`.
- **Failure isolation:** rendering happens only on the scrape path, exceptions are converted to 503, and scrapes plus health probes bypass request instrumentation, so `http_requests_total` and business endpoints are unaffected by a broken exporter. Health and readiness never depend on export.
- **Registry hardening:** `OperationalMetrics.snapshot()` now includes `sum` for observations (additive), and `observe()` rejects NaN and ±Infinity. Label bounds remain `[A-Za-z0-9_.:/-]` and 80 characters, so tenant ids, user ids, tokens, document names and raw request paths cannot become labels.
- **Coverage:** HTTP (`http_requests_total`, `http_request_duration_ms`), document upload and OCR, exchange, report runs, report schedules, storage reconciliation and backup consistency series all export through the same boundary.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_metrics_export.py` (27) covers settings validation and token secrecy, deterministic/escaped/sorted exposition, byte and non-finite rejection, the three states, bearer matching, exporter failure isolation, 404/401/405/503 paths, HEAD, output-limit enforcement, no self-instrumentation and domain metric coverage. Focused suite `tests/test_metrics_export.py` + `tests/test_observability.py` + `tests/test_production_readiness.py` → 57 passed.
- **Verification:** Python 3.12.13, `psycopg` 3.3.9, PostgreSQL 16.2. Compile of changed modules passes. Full SQLite suite → **4036 passed, 170 skipped**. Full live PostgreSQL suite on the same database, twice consecutively → **4260 passed, 1 skipped** both runs. Exact-head CI at `d56152e` all 8 checks **success**: PostgreSQL (`43355779198`), test 3.11/3.12/3.13 (`43355779196`), four typechecks (`43355779296`). Merged `main` verified at `8482775`.
- **Status:** implemented, locally tested, PostgreSQL tested, merged. No Prometheus server, collector, remote-write endpoint or scrape target is **CONFIGURED** in any environment. No credential was created. This is metrics export only; distributed tracing is not implemented and is not claimed.

## Agent 3 checkpoint — Issue #1799, object-storage reconciliation and orphan cleanup — 2026-11-13 (merged; PR #1865)

- **Base and merge:** branch cut from merged `main` `97d6405`, squash-merged as **`c792d19`** via PR #1865. Scope is Workstream B.
- **Reconciler:** `construction_pm.document_storage_reconciliation.DocumentStorageReconciler` classifies `match`, `metadata_only`, `orphan_object`, `checksum_mismatch`, `size_mismatch`, `incomplete_upload`, `pending_deletion`, `unknown_object`, `retention_protected` and `unavailable`, then reports counts plus per-anomaly document id/version/opaque object handle. Only `orphan_object`, `incomplete_upload` and `pending_deletion` are destructive candidates.
- **Safety gates:** the metadata snapshot (content versions, dispositions, policies) is read before the object store is listed, and any metadata/inventory exception returns `status="aborted"` with nothing deleted. Dry-run is the default and writes nothing, including no observation counter. `min_observations` is validated to be at least two, default grace is 24 hours, and the conditional claim re-checks count, grace and lease in one SQL statement. A live version or legal hold protects bytes even when a sibling version was disposed (deduplicated content). Ownership requires the object scope hash to map to exactly one metadata scope; unmapped/ambiguous objects are report-only. The lease commits before the external delete; `deleted`/`failed` outcomes commit immediately with an append-only cleanup row (actor, reason, object handle, checksum, size, status, attempt, timestamp) and failures retry. Report and log output never include storage references, paths, bucket names, credentials or payloads.
- **Adapters and stores:** `LocalDocumentObjectInventory` walks the content-addressed layout and detects `.upload-*` staging files with path containment, symlink rejection and scope re-verification. `S3DocumentObjectInventory` uses signed `ListObjectsV2` pagination added to `S3CompatibleDocumentStorage`; malformed or out-of-prefix keys are `unknown_object`. `SQLiteDocumentStorageReconciliationStore` and `PostgresDocumentStorageReconciliationStore` share DDL/`ON CONFLICT` observation semantics, conditional claims, lease reclaim and immutable cleanup rows. Content and retention stores gained additive cross-document `list_all` / `list_all_policies` / `list_all_dispositions` for both dialects.
- **Tests:** new `tests/test_document_storage_reconciliation.py` (25) covers match, missing object, size and byte-level checksum mismatch, staging, dry-run non-mutation, repeated-observation and grace gates, failed-delete retry, idempotent repeats, reappearance reset, unknown scope, tenant scoping, shared-reference/legal-hold retention, fail-closed aborts, report redaction, unsafe-policy rejection and an S3 stub end-to-end. New `tests/integration/test_storage_reconciliation_postgres_live.py` (3) runs against real PostgreSQL 16.2: durable outcome across connections, two-thread single delete, and exclusive/reclaimable lease. The PostgreSQL workflow now includes the module and live test.
- **Verification:** Python 3.12.13, `psycopg` 3.3.9, PostgreSQL 16.2. Compile of all changed modules passes. Full SQLite suite → **4009 passed, 170 skipped**. Full live PostgreSQL suite on the same database, twice consecutively → **4233 passed, 1 skipped** both runs. Exact-head CI at `c3d701e` all 8 checks **success**: PostgreSQL (`43354320637`), test 3.11/3.12/3.13 (`43354320671`), four typechecks (`43354320634`). Merged `main` verified at `c792d19`.
- **Status:** implemented, locally tested, PostgreSQL tested, merged. Scheduling, operator actor provisioning and production bucket/local-root configuration are **NOT CONFIGURED**; no production bytes were listed or deleted.
- **Residual risk:** content-addressed deduplication leaves a narrow window between snapshot and delete if an upload reuses the same bytes; the two-observation gate plus the 24-hour default grace is the mitigation, and the final delete still re-validates scope and path. Unknown or ambiguous scopes are never cleaned. Workstream D independent security review is still open.


## Agent 3 checkpoint — Issue #1799, PostgreSQL report register and schedules — 2026-11-13 (merged; PR #1863)

- **Base and merge:** branch cut from merged `main` `72a8a5a`, squash-merged as **`90e1543`** via PR #1863. Scope is Workstream A (PostgreSQL report register/schedules) and the schedule lease durability it exposed.
- **Stores:** `PostgresReportStore` and `PostgresReportScheduleStore` mirror the SQLite stores: `%s` placeholders, `TEXT` timestamps/JSON, no implicit commits, additive `CREATE TABLE/INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` plus `information_schema`-guarded `ADD COLUMN` upgrades. Definitions cover idempotency-key replay/reuse, revisions, append-only audit, create/read/list/search/update/delete and tenant/project scoping on every query; runs are immutable snapshots with parameter and parameter-source provenance, duplicate-run rejection and `next_run_id`; schedules cover CRUD with optimistic conflicts, audit history, exclusive conditional claims, `complete`/`fail` claim-token verification and 500-character error bounds.
- **Parity decisions:** PostgreSQL search uses `ILIKE` to match SQLite `LIKE`; definition and schedule writes use conditional `UPDATE`/`DELETE ... WHERE revision = expected` so a racing writer receives `REPORT_REVISION_CONFLICT` / `REPORT_SCHEDULE_REVISION_CONFLICT`; unique violations map to the existing `REPORT_ID_ALREADY_EXISTS`, `REPORT_RUN_ID_ALREADY_EXISTS` and `REPORT_SCHEDULE_ALREADY_EXISTS` codes.
- **Defect found and fixed:** the schedule runner acquired a lease but did not make it durable before execution. On PostgreSQL an inner execution rollback (an idempotent replay of an existing deterministic run id) also rolled back the claim, so `complete` failed with `REPORT_SCHEDULE_CLAIM_LOST` and a second worker could re-run the occurrence. Both stores now expose `commit_claim()`, called by `ReportScheduleRunner` after claiming; SQLite behavior is unchanged apart from the earlier commit point.
- **Tests:** new `tests/integration/test_report_postgres_live.py` (8 tests) runs against real PostgreSQL 16.2: definition lifecycle/idempotency/audit/isolation, immutable runs, schedule CRUD/conflicts/history, two-thread claim exclusivity, lease reclaim and stale-claim rejection, failure backoff/error truncation, transactional rollback with no partial rows, runner execution/replay/retry, and an explicit SQLite↔PostgreSQL `as_dict()` parity check.
- **Verification:** Python 3.12.9, `psycopg` 3.3.9. Compile of all changed modules passes. Full SQLite suite → **3984 passed, 169 skipped**. Full live PostgreSQL suite on the same database, twice consecutively → **4186 passed, 20 skipped** both runs. PR checks all 8 **success**: PostgreSQL (`43351831908`), test 3.11/3.12/3.13 (`43351831917`), four typechecks (`43351831882`).
- **Status:** implemented, locally tested, PostgreSQL tested, merged. Preview runtime still composes SQLite stores; production PostgreSQL composition and production verification **NOT CONFIGURED**. Delivery transport (email/S3/webhook) and delivery-attempt metadata remain outside the current contract.
- **Not claimed:** no production deployment or database change, no external object storage/scanner/OCR/metrics verification, no independent security review and no V26 numeric parity.

## Agent 3 checkpoint — Issue #1799, slice 4 (`agent3/report-parameters-2026-11`, PR pending)

- **Baseline:** `main` @ `bca5800` (PDF export merged as PR #1803; `da22846` is Agent 1's document-bytes workspace merge).
- **Parameterized report definitions (Workstream 3):** a definition can bind a typed subset of the subject's server-owned parameter catalogue. Activities expose `status`, `activity_type`, `actual_start_from` and `actual_start_to`; resources expose `resource_type` and `is_active`; risks expose `status`, `category` and `min_score`. Bindings store only key/required/default; labels, types, enum choices and filter semantics live in `report_parameters.py`, so a client cannot widen an enum or inject a label.
- **Run semantics and provenance:** `POST .../reports/{id}/run` accepts `{"parameters": {...}}`; values are canonicalized and the run stores resolved `parameters` plus `parameter_sources` (`explicit`/`default`). Filtering applies to the stored snapshot rows only — no row, column or aggregate is invented. Missing required, unknown, invalid and duplicate/non-object payloads fail closed with `REPORT_PARAMETER_REQUIRED`, `REPORT_PARAMETER_UNKNOWN`, `REPORT_PARAMETER_INVALID` and `INVALID_REPORT_PARAMETER_LIST`/`REPORT_PARAMETER_DUPLICATED`.
- **Idempotency and revisions:** bindings are part of the definition fingerprint, so reusing a key with changed parameters returns 409 `REPORT_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSE`; a parameter change through update appends a revision and audit event.
- **Migration (Workstream 5):** `SQLiteReportStore.initialize()` adds `parameters_json` to the definition and run tables with empty defaults; a test creates the pre-parameter schema, inserts a definition/run, runs `initialize()`, reads both unchanged, then writes and rereads a parameterized definition.
- **Tests:** new `tests/test_report_parameters.py` (7 tests) covers catalogue exposure, explicit/default provenance, all-subject filtering, required/unknown/invalid/duplicate failures, fingerprint reuse and the SQLite migration. Full suite: **3613 passed, 147 skipped** without a DSN and **3801 passed, 1 skipped** with both PostgreSQL DSN variables.
- **Not claimed:** durable scheduled runs/retry/delivery checksums, a PostgreSQL report store (the register remains SQLite-only) and Web parameter/downstream wiring (Agent 1's surface) remain open.

## Agent 3 checkpoint — Issue #1799, slice 3 (`agent3/reports-pdf-2026-11`, merged as `bca5800`, PR #1803)

- **Baseline:** `main` @ `3f8a67e` (slice 2 merged; PR #1802 squash merge `3f8a67ee659bd7db14bdb9b97b881308f3a71de0`).
- **Report PDF export (Workstream 3, first slice):** `report_pdf.py` renders an immutable stored `ReportRun` as a deterministic A4 PDF through `build_report_export(run, "pdf", locale=...)`. Every page repeats subject, report id, run id, project id, report revision, project revision, generated-at and actor; the table header repeats after page breaks; the footer carries `Page N of M` and the run id. Cells wrap, an oversized cell fails closed with `REPORT_EXPORT_PDF_CELL_TOO_LONG` instead of truncating, an empty run renders an explicit notice, and `reportlab` runs with a fixed timestamp plus fixed metadata so the same run+locale yields byte-identical output and `X-Content-SHA256`.
- **Locales and RTL:** `en` renders Gregorian ISO dates and grouped numbers; `fa` renders Jalali dates, Persian digits (`٬` grouping, `٫` decimal), the `Direction = R2L` viewer preference and a document language tag. Persian/Arabic text is shaped with `arabic-reshaper` and reordered with `python-bidi` in both locales; identifiers keep their stored Latin digits.
- **Fonts and licensing:** two glyph subsets of DejaVu Sans (Latin, Arabic, presentation forms, punctuation) ship under `src/construction_pm/assets/fonts/` with the Bitstream Vera license; they are registered as `CubiSans`/`CubiSans-Bold` (rename required for modified copies). Runtime dependencies now declare `reportlab>=4.4,<5`, `arabic-reshaper>=3.0,<4` and `python-bidi>=0.6,<0.7`; `pypdf` is a test-only dependency.
- **HTTP contract:** `GET .../reports/{report_id}/runs/{run_id}/export?format=csv|xlsx|pdf&locale=en|fa` keeps the existing `Content-Disposition`, `X-Content-SHA256` and `X-Report-Run` headers; the catalogue advertises `export_formats` and `export_locales`. Unsupported format/locale are 400; unknown run 404; cross-scope 403. Recorded in `docs/engineering/P6_REPORT_EXPORT_CONTRACT.md`.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_report_export.py` now covers byte determinism, identity/metadata, multi-page pagination with repeated headers and correct page numbering, empty runs, Jalali/Persian-digit/RTL output and format/locale/column failures, plus HTTP download headers and both locales. Full suite: **3606 passed, 147 skipped** without a DSN; **3794 passed, 1 skipped** with both PostgreSQL DSN variables, repeated twice consecutively on the same database with identical results.
- **Repeatability fix (Workstream 5):** `tests/test_p6_code_assignment_postgres.py` committed a fixed primary key in its third test, so later runs against the same database failed the immutable-metadata tests. Rows now use a per-run UUID; two consecutive runs pass (3 passed each).
- **Not claimed:** parameterized definitions with typed provenance, durable scheduled runs/retry/delivery checksums, Web download wiring (Agent 1 owns the Web surface), and XML register exchange remain open.

## Agent 3 checkpoint — Issue #1799, slice 2 (`agent3/exchange-datasets-2026-11`, PR pending)

- **Baseline:** `main` @ `034d0a3` (slice 1 merged).
- **Exchange import/export (Workstream 2):** `p6-exchange-api.v1` now exposes six datasets: `activities`, `resources`, `risks`, `wbs`, `assignments`, `costs`. Activities, resources, WBS, assignments and costs are importable; `risks` remains export-only (`EXCHANGE_DATASET_READ_ONLY`). Exports are CSV (default) or XLSX with typed numeric/date cells and stable ordering; every import format uses the same header/arity/type/enum/duplicate/parent/cycle/cross-reference/revision/idempotency pipeline. Quoted fields, embedded delimiters and multiline cells are parsed with `csv.DictReader`, never string splitting.
- **Atomic apply:** apply requires a transaction manager, validates the full batch before the first write, applies WBS/resources parents-first and other registers in deterministic ID order through the canonical APIs (`P6WBSMasterAPI`, `P6ActivityMasterAPI`, `P6ResourceAPI`, `P6ResourceAssignmentApplicationService`, `P6ExpenseApplicationService`), and records the run plus idempotency key inside the same transaction. Any write failure rolls the entire batch back and records an `EXCHANGE_APPLY_FAILED` run without reserving the key. `applied.replayed` remains true only for a same-key/same-payload replay; same key with different content is 409 `EXCHANGE_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSE`.
- **PostgreSQL parity:** `PostgresExchangeStore` mirrors the SQLite run/idempotency schema and is covered by `tests/integration/test_exchange_postgres_live.py` for successful apply, replay, key reuse and whole-batch rollback.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_exchange_registers.py` covers WBS preview/apply/export, activity/assignment/cost round-trip, XLSX WBS import, invalid references, quoted/multiline CSV and atomic rollback; `tests/test_exchange_api.py` is updated for the expanded catalogue. Full suite: **3600 passed, 147 skipped** without a DSN and **3769 passed, 20 skipped** with PostgreSQL (0 failed).
- **Explicit gate:** XML register exchange is not implemented because no versioned XML domain contract exists in this repository; implementing one would invent a second schema. Project-to-project/remote exchange remains out of scope until an approved transfer contract exists.
- **Still open in Issue #1799:** report PDF/parameterized/scheduled output, object-storage/quarantine/OCR adapters, migration parity audit, configuration/health hardening.

## Documents binary-content checkpoint — 2026-11-12 (`81f457a9`, branch `feat/documents-binary-v26`)

- **Scope:** closes the Web half of GAP-DOC-02. The Documents window no longer asks users for a storage reference and hash as the only path: the Content tab accepts a file, computes SHA-256 in the browser, uploads the bytes through `POST .../documents/{id}/content?expected_revision=…` with one idempotency key per chosen digest, and refuses success if the response digest differs. Stored versions are listed from `.../content/versions`; each download verifies the `X-Content-SHA256` header against the received bytes before saving. Activity Details WPs & Docs now loads the authoritative document register lazily and filters it by activity link instead of reading the dead bootstrap `documents: []` source. Transport additions are `postBytes`/`download` in `client.ts`; permissions remain server-enforced (`viewer` downloads only).

Browser E2E `apps/web/e2e/documents-binary-v26.mjs` passed 8/8 (create+upload v1, byte-identical verified download, immutable v2 while v1 is unchanged, server rejection of PDF-declared HTML with `DOCUMENT_CONTENT_TYPE_MISMATCH`, reload persistence, viewer download-only, 1280×720 fa RTL, 1366×768 and 1920×1080 fit) with zero exceptions, console errors or API failures. The pre-existing metadata regression `apps/web/e2e/documents-v26.mjs` passed 13/13 on the same commit. Evidence: `docs/workspace/evidence/documents-binary-v26-2026-11-12/` and refreshed `docs/workspace/evidence/documents-v26-2026-11-12/`; both JSON reports record `testedCodeSha`/`evidenceCommitSha` `81f457a9`.

## Review-split checkpoint — 2026-10-26 (layout-concurrency fix `4b140c89` plus the evidence head on PR #1583; full-stack preview and Web slices; not merged)

- **Branch:** `feat/full-stack-preview-workspace`, split from the over-coupled PR #1582 so the preview/Web delivery can be reviewed and merged independently. It carries the preview runtime, formula validation panel, interchange panel, typed effective-dated capacity, typed Jalali entry, durable layout history and explicit revision restore. The Resource Dependent placement and AI execution-verification work remains on PR #1582 (`fix/resource-dependent-nondriving-leveling`) and is deliberately not claimed here. Review PR: #1583.
- **Typed Jalali entry:** DATE_BASED schedule settings render the persisted start in the selected display calendar, accept a typed Jalali date, convert it to the canonical ISO value on the server, and fail closed with `INVALID_PROJECT_START` for impossible dates without mutating stored data. Time-aware settings keep the ISO control; other workspace date cells remain read-only display values.
- **Durable layout history:** the layout repository/API/HTTP route exposes `GET .../p6/layouts/{scope}/{view}/revisions` as an append-only, newest-first `p6-layout-definition-api.v1` envelope with read-permission and scope checks. The Web field chooser loads that history, offers Undo/Redo with localized notices, and a revision picker that restores any older revision by saving it as a new head revision. Every undo, redo and restore appends a revision; prior rows are never rewritten, and undo remains available after a full browser reload. Layout notices survive the asynchronous history prefetch so the outcome is not erased before it can be read.
- **Concurrency review fix (`4b140c89`):** the store previously compared a two-field slice with the three-field encoded row, so an identical retry never matched and stale writers were always labelled immutable. A single `_decide_write` helper now classifies retries, appends, immutable revisions and stale bases for both SQLite and PostgreSQL; a matching retry writes nothing, a stale base or revision gap returns `REVISION_CONFLICT`, and changing an already-published revision returns `IMMUTABLE_LAYOUT_REVISION`. The layout POST maps both to HTTP 409 with `error.p6.layout.conflict`. The Web client reloads the authoritative head for either code and shows the localized conflict notice instead of dropping history as a generic failure. Typed Jalali/datetime input now clears a stale canonical ISO value when parsing fails.
- **Local verification on this split branch:** full Python `2781 passed, 126 skipped, 2 warnings` (the RD/AI-specific tests are intentionally absent); focused layout repository/API/HTTP/PostgreSQL-twin `100 passed, 1 skipped`; Web `323` TypeScript tests plus `7` preview-resolver tests; `npm run typecheck` and `npm run build` passed; isolated browser E2E `36 checks, 25 screenshots` on `bde7ef73` (the artifacts' `evidence.json` records that `git_head`) with temporary SQLite and random loopback port, including undo → redo → restore older revision → two competing-writer conflict branches → undo after reload with server revision assertions, `04c-layout-restore.png`, `04d-layout-conflict.png` and `04e-layout-conflict-gap.png`. The same-revision branch publishes a competing revision (head 12→13) and the stale UI write receives `IMMUTABLE_LAYOUT_REVISION`; the gap branch advances the head by two (stale base 14, authoritative head 16) and receives `REVISION_CONFLICT`. In both, the UI reloads the authoritative head, keeps the competing presentation and appends nothing. The invalid-Jalali path is also checked after switching calendars to prove no stale ISO resurrection. PostgreSQL execution is covered by repository CI modules; the layout PostgreSQL twin skips locally because `P6_TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` is unset.
- **Exact-head CI:** all four workflows and nine checks succeeded on `bde7ef73` (E2E conflict-gap and evidence head): [ConstructionPM CI #40333341662](https://github.com/consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform/actions/runs/40333341662) (Python 3.11/3.12/3.13), [PostgreSQL Integration #40333341392](https://github.com/consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform/actions/runs/40333341392), [ConstructionPM Web CI #40333341654](https://github.com/consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform/actions/runs/40333341654), [Client Typecheck #40333341526](https://github.com/consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform/actions/runs/40333341526). The final documentation-only head repeats these workflows; PR #1583 records that exact-head run. Earlier head `d8840a20` also passed 9/9 ([Python](https://github.com/consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform/actions/runs/40329629363), [PostgreSQL](https://github.com/consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform/actions/runs/40329629302), [Web](https://github.com/consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform/actions/runs/40329629053), [Client typecheck](https://github.com/consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform/actions/runs/40329629082)). These certify branch heads, not merged `main`.
- **Independent review:** two review rounds found no remaining code blocker. The first found the layout retry/stale-write defect and the single-code Web recovery; both are fixed and covered at repository, PostgreSQL-twin, HTTP and browser layers. The second found documentation overclaiming (AI-05/07/08 and stale OPS rows) and a missing browser branch for the revision-gap conflict; both are corrected in the evidence commit, whose matrix marks AI-07 `missing` and AI-05/AI-08 `partial` on this tree because the verification module and AI browser E2E remain on PR #1582.

## Product-owner decision — single canonical Visual Workspace (2026-11-04)

- **Decision:** CUBI has exactly one main product environment: the Visual Workspace. No separate dashboard, generic authenticated SaaS app, alternate workspace shell or separate visual-demo/help route is permitted.
- **Public site order:** Home → normal public/informational pages (Platform, Solutions, Resources, Pricing, Contact, etc.) → one clear product/login entry point to the Visual Workspace.
- **Session flow:** successful login opens the Visual Workspace by default (or restores a requested deep link inside that same shell); logout returns to public Home.
- **Capability placement:** all projects, engineering engines, tools, reports, forms, settings and administration are windows/pages/menus/tabs/panels/dialogs inside the one persistent Visual Workspace.
- **No-regression:** preserve existing approved workspace dimensions, layout, navigation and functions. This decision does not authorize a redesign, removal of existing pages, or changes to the Gantt sizing.
- **Governance:** issue #1712 is the P0 acceptance gate. Visual shell audit and workspace IA now reference this product-surface rule. Keep the issue open until the running website proves public navigation → login → single workspace → logout → Home and no competing app shell exists.
- **Documentation change baseline:** branch `docs/single-visual-workspace-product-principle`, based on main SHA `3214e3c8cd204d772ae641dd489a97c781c87e59`. Documentation-only changes; no runtime/UI code changed. Exact branch head and PR checks are recorded in the PR when opened.

## Canonical workspace and multilingual audit — 2026-11-04

**Product invariant:** the authenticated Visual Workspace is the only product environment. Public informational pages remain separate; every authenticated project tool/window must live inside the one persistent workspace. Preserve the approved shell geometry and Gantt dimensions.

## Knowledge assistant V1 branch checkpoint — 2026-11-12 (`feature/cubi-knowledge-assistant-v1`; not merged) — SUPERSEDED by the post-merge checkpoint below

> **Historical record, superseded.** The statements below that PR #1794 is "open, mergeable, unmerged" and that no merged-main completion exists are accurate only for the branch checkpoint they describe. PR #1794 merged on 2026-11-12T14:31:38Z; see "Knowledge assistant V1 POST-MERGE CHECKPOINT" immediately after this section for the current state. The original text is preserved unedited for history.

- **Base and scope:** branch cut from `fe3f55c66de3570cdddf199fa0c997a4441420fd`; implementation commit `1be0573`, governance commit `859a282`, CI-evidence commit `ce09980`, pushed to `origin/feature/cubi-knowledge-assistant-v1`; PR #1794 targets `main`. This is branch evidence, not merged-main completion. It adds an independent knowledge/assistant/workflow slice under `src/construction_pm/knowledge/` plus `tools/ingest_v26_help.py` and `tools/knowledge_multilingual_evidence.py`, with no change to the Visual Workspace Shell, Activities/Gantt workspaces, left/right/horizontal toolbars, layout selector, Activity Details, Gantt context menus, workspace layout, shared toolbar CSS/theme or shell icon registry.
- **Shared-file changes:** `src/construction_pm/application/authorization.py` gains five permissions (`knowledge.read`, `knowledge.contribute`, `knowledge.review`, `knowledge.publish`, `assistant.action`) and role grants; `tests/test_administration_api.py` updates the expected default grant sets. No other tracked shared file is modified.
- **Official V26 extraction:** exactly the two authoritative HTML sources are ingestable and were fetched live: HTML Help `client_help/en_US/toc.htm` 982/982 and User Guide `English/User_Guides/p6_pro_user/toc.htm` 557/557 — **1,539 topics, 0 failed**. All topics carry a stable `(source_id, topic_key)`, 64-character content hash, source URL, TOC depth/order and parent key. Re-running is idempotent and resumes at page boundaries; HTTP fetches may run in parallel while parsing and writes remain ordered. 509 User Guide topics are content-identical to an HTML Help card and are merged onto the canonical card with a `topic_merged` audit record while both source URLs remain in the topic records; 1,030 unique draft cards are retained. The non-ingestable registry entries (`books.html`, docs home and the legacy Professional Help path) are not fetched. The supplementary 815-page PDF is recorded **MISSING / VERIFY**; no page from it is claimed.
- **Knowledge quality:** TOC titles are authoritative, chapter ordinals inside `span.enumeration_*` are excluded from titles, `p.subhead1/2` is promoted to section headings, and cover `h2.docinfo` metadata no longer becomes a topic title. Stub pages produce an explicit "no body content" limitation instead of a fabricated procedure; 1,478 cards contain extracted steps. Cards remain structurally validated drafts; none is published or human-reviewed.
- **Multilingual model:** ten locales (nine working languages plus source English; `zh-CN` and `zh-TW` are distinct), per-locale translation lifecycle with independent status/review/publish/retire and explicit missing/draft/translated/review-required/approved/published/retired states. Script-aware tokenization covers CJK without whitespace, Russian inflectional forms, and Persian/Arabic orthography with tatweel and diacritic handling; BM25 is preserved and both query and document expansion support cross-language retrieval. The assistant detects question language, responds in the selected or requested language, preserves card ids and source URLs, marks borrowed fields, and never presents an unapproved translation as published. Translations record contributor identity and source, revisions preserve the replaced content in audit detail, and seed translations exist for nine locales as `translated`/`pending`; only Russian is published in the disposable smoke copy.
- **Services:** 10 card types with Draft → review → publish → retire lifecycle and revision/event history; central/project/user-memory scope isolation; three assistant modes (training answer, persisted Smart Guide, approved real execution); ordered project-creation workflow with 13 guidance items, optimistic `expected_version`, idempotent `request_key`, dependency validation, back/skip rules, interruption adoption, approval token bound to `run_id|actor|review_hash`, and injected real lifecycle/plan ports that fail closed. A WSGI middleware owns only `/api/knowledge*` and `/api/projects/{id}/knowledge` and delegates all other paths.
- **Local verification:** `python -m pytest -q tests/knowledge` → **182 passed**. Full Python suite → **3,778 passed, 116 skipped, 3 pre-existing unknown-mark warnings**.
- **Evidence:** `docs/workspace/evidence/knowledge-multilingual-v26-2026-11-12/` contains `cubi-knowledge-v26.sqlite3`, `coverage.json`, `ingestion-report.json`, `ingest.log`, `source-audit.json`, `translation-matrix.json`, `assistant-smoke.json`, `seed-report.json` and `SOURCE_STATUS.md`. Architecture: `docs/architecture/CUBI_KNOWLEDGE_AND_ASSISTANT_V1.md`.
- **Exact-head CI:** head `859a2829e42f111642667c8d329a22c90766a3c3` passed all 8 checks: Python 3.11/3.12/3.13 and PostgreSQL ([run 43225529135](https://github.com/consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform/actions/runs/43225529135), [run 43225529131](https://github.com/consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform/actions/runs/43225529131)) and client-sync/Web/mobile/desktop typecheck ([run 43225529452](https://github.com/consttruction-pm/consttruction-pm-platform/actions/runs/43225529452)). ## Durable report schedules slice — 2026-11-12 (merged; PR #1805)

- **Base:** branch cut from merged `main` `842208d` (parameterized report definitions/runs); squash-merged to `main` as `89b1d1d` via PR #1805. Scope is Issue #1799 Workstream 3: durable scheduled report jobs, without a second scheduler and without transport delivery that needs external credentials.
- **Durable model:** new `project_report_schedule` table stores cadence (`daily|weekly|monthly`), local `run_time`, IANA `timezone`, `weekday`/`day_of_month`, `project_revision`, `export_format`, `locale`, explicit `parameters`, `enabled`, optimistic `revision`, `next_run_at`, `last_run_at`, `last_status`, `last_error`, `attempts`, claim token/lease and timestamps. `SQLiteReportScheduleStore.initialize()` also adds `project_revision` to databases created by the earlier untracked build, so no existing database is dropped or rebuilt.
- **Next occurrence:** `compute_next_run` converts local wall clock through `zoneinfo` to UTC with strict `>` semantics; DST gaps map forward and repeated times use `fold=0`. Covered for daily/weekly/monthly and both 2026 New York DST transitions.
- **Execution:** `ReportScheduleRunner.run_due(now, worker_id, limit)` claims enabled due rows with a random token and lease. Run identity is deterministic (`RUN-SCH-{schedule_id}-{scheduled_for UTC}`) and `generated_at` is the scheduled instant, so a crash after the immutable run is saved but before completion reuses it instead of duplicating it. Success records the export SHA-256, resets attempts and advances the cadence; failure records bounded error text, increments attempts and retries with exponential backoff (5-minute base, 60-minute cap). A re-claimed lease can no longer complete/fail the row (`REPORT_SCHEDULE_CLAIM_LOST`).
- **Snapshot semantics:** a schedule captures `project_revision` at create/update and materializes at that revision. Only explicit parameter values are stored; defaults are re-resolved at execution, so the run records real `explicit`/`default` provenance. Scheduled materialization goes through a new trusted `ReportApplicationService.run_scheduled(...)`; it revalidates inputs and definition existence but is not an HTTP authorization bypass (no user context exists on the deployment trigger).
- **HTTP/API:** `GET|POST .../reports/{id}/schedules`, `GET|PUT|PATCH|DELETE .../schedules/{schedule_id}`, `GET .../schedules/{schedule_id}/history`. Reads need project-read; writes need project-write and same scope; stale updates/deletes are `409 REPORT_SCHEDULE_REVISION_CONFLICT`; duplicates are `409 REPORT_SCHEDULE_ALREADY_EXISTS`. Catalogue advertises `schedule_cadences`.
- **Audit separation:** schedule events use `entity_type = 'report_schedule'`; `definition_history` now filters `entity_type = 'report'`, so schedule events cannot appear in report history. Audit revisions use the next free key, so delete → re-create with the same id remains append-only instead of colliding.
- **Contract:** `docs/engineering/P6_REPORT_SCHEDULES_CONTRACT.md`; the export contract points to it instead of listing scheduled runs as out of scope.
- **Verification:** `tests/test_report_schedules.py` → **6 passed** (next-run/DST, CRUD/revision/audit/re-create, execute-once + saved-run reuse + checksum, lease reclaim + stale-claim rejection + disabled schedule, failure/backoff/recovery, HTTP CRUD/authorization/history separation). Full local Python suite (SQLite) → **3,801 passed, 147 skipped, 3 pre-existing unknown-mark warnings**. Full local suite with a live `pgserver` DSN (`P6_TEST_POSTGRES_DSN` and `CONSTRUCTION_PM_POSTGRES_DSN`) → **3,989 passed, 1 skipped**.
- **Not claimed:** no in-process timer or second scheduler; no email/S3/webhook delivery, destination configuration or delivery retry (external credentials); no Web/Visual Workspace schedule UI (Agent 1); no PostgreSQL schedule/report store; no deployment.
- **Exact-head CI and merge:** PR #1805 head `9c62b57` passed all 7 reported checks (ConstructionPM CI Python 3.11/3.12/3.13 with the PostgreSQL service, client-sync/Web/mobile/desktop typecheck). Squash merge `89b1d1d`; the resulting `main` run for ConstructionPM CI, PostgreSQL Sync State Integration and Client Typecheck all completed `success`.

## Document storage, scan quarantine and S3 adapter — 2026-11-12 (merged; PR #1808)

- **Base:** branch cut from `c134bb9` and rebased onto merged `main` `b989d3e` before opening PR; squash-merged to `main` as `6daaf12` via PR #1808. Scope is Issue #1799 Workstream 4 (document object storage, malware-scanning interface with quarantine-first behavior), excluding OCR and retention, which remain separate slices.
- **Scan contract:** new `document_scan.py` defines `SCAN_STATUSES` (`clean|infected|failed|unscanned`), `ScanVerdict` validation, a `DocumentScanner` protocol and the deterministic `ReferenceDocumentScanner`. The reference scanner recognises only the public EICAR marker and is explicitly a test/development adapter, not an antivirus.
- **Quarantine-first upload:** `DocumentApplicationService` accepts `scanner` and `require_scanned_content`. Scanning happens after payload validation and before `storage.put`, so infected payloads are rejected in both modes and no object or version is written. In strict mode a missing scanner, scanner exception, `failed` verdict or `unscanned` verdict fails closed with no write. In non-strict development mode the version is recorded as `failed`/`unscanned` and is never presented as clean.
- **Quarantine-first download:** strict mode serves only a selected version with `scan_status == "clean"`; other versions and legacy metadata-only `storage_ref` downloads return `409 DOCUMENT_CONTENT_QUARANTINED` without reading bytes. Infected upload returns `409 DOCUMENT_CONTENT_INFECTED`; scan unavailable/failed returns `503`.
- **Version metadata:** `DocumentContentVersion` now carries `scan_status`, `scan_engine`, `scan_engine_version`, `scan_detail` and exposes a `scan` object in `as_dict()`. `_to_json` persists all four; `_from_json` defaults legacy rows to `unscanned` with empty engine fields, so no table rebuild is needed. SQLite and PostgreSQL repositories share the serializer; the live Postgres test now covers both legacy defaulting and clean-verdict round-trip.
- **Shared object keys:** `storage_scope_key` and `document_object_parts` derive `{scope(tenant)}/{scope(project)}/{digest[0:2]}/{digest}{extension}` from server values only. Extensions are now restricted to the validated content-type allow-list. Both local and S3 adapters resolve references through the same scope/hash checks.
- **S3-compatible adapter:** `S3CompatibleDocumentStorage` signs with SigV4 over stdlib `urllib`, supports path-style and virtual-host URLs, optional `AES256`/`aws:kms` SSE and session tokens, re-verifies size and digest on read, maps `404` to `DOCUMENT_CONTENT_NOT_FOUND`, other HTTP/transport failures to `DOCUMENT_STORAGE_OBJECT_UNAVAILABLE`, and makes `DELETE` idempotent. There is no vendor SDK dependency.
- **Explicit production configuration:** `build_document_storage_from_environment` selects `CONSTRUCTION_PM_DOCUMENT_STORAGE=local|s3`. Production with no selection fails `DOCUMENT_STORAGE_CONFIGURATION_REQUIRED`; production `local` fails `DOCUMENT_STORAGE_LOCAL_FORBIDDEN_IN_PRODUCTION`; S3 requires `CONSTRUCTION_PM_S3_*` values and missing-variable errors list names only. Prefix segments `.`/`..` are rejected.
- **Composition:** the preview runtime injects `ReferenceDocumentScanner()` with `require_scanned_content=True`, so browser uploads/downloads exercise the quarantine path.
- **Contract:** `docs/engineering/P6_DOCUMENT_STORAGE_CONTRACT.md` documents references, the scan matrix, HTTP codes, S3 settings and the external/out-of-scope list.
- **Verification:** `tests/test_document_scan_and_object_storage.py` → **26 passed** (scanner contract and invalid verdicts, legacy JSON defaulting, strict clean upload/download, infected rejection with no object write, missing/broken/failed scanner fail-closed, dev-mode failed/unscanned labelling, version and legacy-reference quarantine, HTTP 409/503 mapping, scoped key derivation, S3 SigV4 recomputation, cross-scope/tamper/404/availability behavior, configuration fail-closed). Full local Python suite (SQLite) → **3,827 passed, 147 skipped, 3 pre-existing unknown-mark warnings**. Full local suite with live `pgserver` DSNs → **4,015 passed, 1 skipped**.
- **Not claimed:** no real antivirus engine, engine licence/credentials or quarantine review UI; no OCR/text-extraction pipeline; no retention/legal-hold/purge lifecycle; no bucket provisioning, lifecycle policy or deployed object store; no Web/Visual Workspace document UI (Agent 1); no production deployment.
- **Exact-head CI and merge:** PR #1808 head `0f48d78` passed all 7 reported checks (ConstructionPM CI Python 3.11/3.12/3.13 with the PostgreSQL service, client-sync/Web/mobile/desktop typecheck). Squash merge `6daaf12`; the resulting `main` runs for ConstructionPM CI, PostgreSQL Sync State Integration and Client Typecheck all completed `success`.

## Document OCR durable queue — 2026-11-12 (merged; PR #1810)

- **Base:** branch cut from merged `main` `835c1fc` (ledger for PR #1808); squash-merged to `main` as `b4d8279` via PR #1810. Scope is Issue #1799 Workstream 4 OCR queueing, excluding a real extraction engine.
- **Compatibility:** the earlier `DocumentOCRAdapter`/`OCRRequest`/`OCRResult`/`ReferenceDocumentOCRAdapter` contract from PR #1745 is preserved unchanged, so `tests/test_document_ocr.py` still passes; the durable queue is additive.
- **Queue model:** new `project_document_ocr` keyed by `(tenant_id, project_id, document_id, version)` stores a snapshot of immutable content facts, status (`pending|running|succeeded|failed|skipped`), attempts/max, `next_attempt_at`, claim token/lease/worker, extracted text/char count and engine identity. A successful strict-mode upload of a `clean` version enqueues one row inside the upload transaction; requests are idempotent.
- **Execution:** `DocumentOcrRunner.run_due` claims due rows with a 300-second lease, reclaims expired leases, rejects stale tokens (`DOCUMENT_OCR_CLAIM_LOST`), records unsupported types as `skipped`, and applies exponential backoff (5 minutes doubling to 60) until terminal `failed`. `retry` accepts only `failed|skipped` and resets attempts; other statuses return `409 DOCUMENT_OCR_NOT_RETRYABLE`.
- **Engine boundary:** `ReferenceDocumentOcrEngine` decodes already-textual `text/plain`, CSV and XML only and raises `OcrUnsupported` for PDF/office/images; it performs no network call. A 200,000-character output limit fails the job instead of truncating evidence.
- **Serving and authorization:** queueing needs project-write and a `clean` scan verdict (`409 DOCUMENT_CONTENT_QUARANTINED` otherwise); reading needs project-read; the actor must match the session.
- **HTTP/parity:** `POST|GET .../content/{version}/ocr`, `GET .../ocr`, `POST .../content/{version}/ocr/retry` return `202` for enqueue/retry, `200` for reads, `404 DOCUMENT_OCR_NOT_FOUND` and `409 DOCUMENT_OCR_NOT_RETRYABLE`. SQLite and PostgreSQL repositories share SQL and serialization.
- **Contract:** `docs/engineering/P6_DOCUMENT_OCR_CONTRACT.md`.
- **Verification:** `tests/test_document_ocr_pipeline.py` → **10 passed** (engine boundary, auto-enqueue/idempotency, quarantine gating, lease reclaim/stale token, runner success/skip, retry/backoff, terminal failure/requeue, text limit, HTTP request/read/list/retry/auth) plus a live PostgreSQL queue test. Full local Python suite (SQLite) → **3,837 passed, 148 skipped**; full live `pgserver` suite → **4,026 passed, 1 skipped**.
- **Not claimed:** no real OCR engine, language packs, confidence/layout metadata or engine credentials; extracted text is only what the reference decoder can read from already-textual payloads. No search indexing, Web UI or deployment.
- **Exact-head CI and merge:** PR #1810 head `a40c047` passed all 7 reported checks; squash merge `b4d8279`; the resulting `main` runs for ConstructionPM CI, PostgreSQL Sync State Integration and Client Typecheck all completed `success`.

## Document retention and deliberate disposition — 2026-11-12 (merged; PR #1816)

- **Base:** branch cut from merged `main` `b4d8279`; squash-merged to `main` as `72741ef` via PR #1816. Scope is Issue #1799 Workstream 4 retention and deletion lifecycle.
- **Policy model:** new `project_document_retention` keyed per document stores `retain_days` (0–36500), `legal_hold`, reason, actor, timestamp and an optimistic `revision`. Creation expects revision 0 and a lost create race conflicts; updates use `UPDATE ... WHERE revision` and stale revisions return `409 DOCUMENT_RETENTION_REVISION_CONFLICT`. Each update appends a `retention_updated` document audit event.
- **Eligibility:** computed per content version as `no_policy`, `legal_hold`, `not_yet_due`, `disposed` or `eligible` from the policy, immutable `uploaded_at` and existing dispositions. Nothing is inferred from a cached flag and no timer runs.
- **Disposition:** `dispose` requires project-write, `confirm == "{document_id}:{version}"`, an existing policy, no legal hold, an existing version, no prior disposition and `occurred_at >= uploaded_at + retain_days`. The storage adapter deletes the object bytes first (missing object tolerated for retry), then an append-only `project_document_disposition` row records hash/reason/actor/time. Metadata and history remain; a second attempt returns `409 DOCUMENT_CONTENT_ALREADY_DISPOSED`.
- **Download refusal:** `DocumentApplicationService.download_content` rejects a disposed version with `409 DOCUMENT_CONTENT_DISPOSED` before reading bytes, independent of scan status.
- **HTTP/parity:** `GET|PUT .../documents/{id}/retention` and `POST .../content/{version}/dispose`, mapped to 200/400/403/404/409/503. SQLite and PostgreSQL stores share SQL, serialization and `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` create protection; byte deletion works for local and S3-compatible adapters through the same port.
- **Composition:** the preview runtime initializes the SQLite retention store, injects it as `disposition_repository` and exposes `DocumentRetentionService`.
- **Contract:** `docs/engineering/P6_DOCUMENT_RETENTION_CONTRACT.md`.
- **Verification:** `tests/test_document_retention.py` → **9 passed** (policy create/update/revision conflict, validation/missing document, legal hold, no policy, confirmation/maturity, byte deletion/download refusal/idempotency, viewer read vs writer writes, HTTP policy/read/dispose/409/400/403) plus a live PostgreSQL policy/disposition parity test. Full local Python suite (SQLite) → **3,846 passed, 149 skipped**; full live `pgserver` suite → **4,017 passed, 20 skipped**.
- **Not claimed:** no scheduler or automatic purge, no litigation/matter workflow beyond the boolean hold, no purge of OCR text/derived previews/backups/object-store lifecycle rules, no retention UI and no deployment.
- **Exact-head CI and merge:** PR #1816 head `d66c430` passed all 7 reported checks; squash merge `72741ef`; the resulting `main` runs for ConstructionPM CI, PostgreSQL Sync State Integration and Client Typecheck all completed `success`.

## Knowledge technical-debt closure — 2026-11-12 (implemented locally; push blocked)

- **Base:** branch `fix/knowledge-technical-debt-2026-11-12` cut from actual `origin/main` `25096d0` (PR #1823). Commits `5d6bf80` (concurrency, history, idempotency, identity, merging, audit triggers, error mapping) and `ab3277e` (inventory tool/evidence and Agent 1 contract). **Not pushed and not merged in this session:** the sandbox had no GitHub credential (`git push` → `could not read Username`), so no PR exists for these commits. They are complete and green locally; publication is the only blocked step.
- **A1 card concurrency:** `save_card(..., expected_version=…)` compares the stored `revision` and raises `KNOWLEDGE_CARD_VERSION_CONFLICT` instead of overwriting a newer review/publication/retirement. `expected_version: 0` means insert-only. Every superseded row is written to `knowledge_card_revision` before the update.
- **A2 translation concurrency:** `save_translation(..., expected_version=…)` compares `translation_version` (`KNOWLEDGE_TRANSLATION_VERSION_CONFLICT`). State transitions now advance the version — content 1 → approval 2 → publication 3 → retirement 4 — so two reviewers or a publication racing an approval can no longer overwrite each other undetected. `published_language_version` records the version created by the publication.
- **A3 idempotency:** `request_key` is bound to a fingerprint of workflow key, tenant, user and canonical locale. Same key + same payload (active, abandoned or completed) returns the stored run; same key + different payload returns `WORKFLOW_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`. The run id is derived from the key, and a concurrent insert that loses the race replays the winner instead of creating a second run. The fingerprint lives in the persisted workflow state, so it survives restart.
- **A4 duplicate merging:** the merge signature no longer contains the page filename or `card_type`, and it now includes the purpose and every content list. Pages that only share a heading are kept as separate topics; identical content across roots is still merged with `duplicate_topic_id`, `duplicate_url` provenance in the audit. The 509 historical merges are preserved as history and are not re-split.
- **A5 card identity:** `card_id_for(source_id, topic_key)` no longer includes the classification, so a reclassified topic keeps one card and its reviewed history. Schema version 3 migrates legacy ids, moving events, translations and revisions with the card; when the target id already exists the legacy row is left untouched instead of overwriting reviewed content.
- **A6 history reads:** `GET /api/knowledge/cards/{id}/history?limit=&before=` returns current revision, superseded revision snapshots and a newest-first event page with `event_id`, `action`, `actor_id`, `occurred_at`, `detail` and a `next_before` cursor. `GET …/translations/{locale}/history` returns translation versions. Draft history requires review permission.
- **A7 audit integrity:** SQLite triggers reject `UPDATE` and `DELETE` on `knowledge_audit` and `knowledge_card_event` (`KNOWLEDGE_AUDIT_IMMUTABLE`, `KNOWLEDGE_CARD_EVENT_IMMUTABLE`). The identity migration is the only writer allowed to move event rows: it drops and recreates the two event triggers inside the migration transaction. This is a SQLite-level boundary; the PostgreSQL store has not been extended and remains application-boundary only.
- **A8 error consistency:** HTTP status is derived from the error code — `*_NOT_FOUND`/`*_NOT_PUBLISHED` → 404, `*_VERSION_CONFLICT`/`*_IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`/`*_ALREADY_*`/`*_CONFLICT` → 409, validation → 400, authorization → 403, missing session → 401. Error bodies are `{"code", "message_key"}`. The version guard runs before the lifecycle transition, so a stale publication now reports 409 rather than a misleading "not approved".
- **Verification (local, SQLite):** `tests/knowledge` → **224 passed**; full suite → **3910 passed, 120 skipped**.
