## RES-004 resource usage availability — 2026-11-14

- **Requirement:** the Resource window must expose usage, availability, limits and overallocation from the authoritative time-phased data, without inventing bucket widths or doing schedule/leveling math in the browser.
- **Implementation (`35f2283a`):** the Resources window gains a lazy Usage tab. The pure `apps/web/src/resource-usage.ts` groups stored assignment periods by `period_start` and sums units/cost as exact decimals; it combines each assignment's stored `units_per_time`, `units_per_time_period` and `hours_per_units_time_period` into comparable, missing, partial or mixed (never converting between bases). `apps/web/src/workspace-resources.ts` resolves each distinct period/basis/hours key through the existing `POST /p6/resource-capacities/{resource_id}/resolve` contract and compares the canonical decimal capacity with stored demand. Statuses cover overallocated, within capacity, missing/partial rate, mixed bases, no effective Max Units, conflicting segments and capacity errors; the excess is exact decimal text.
- **Boundary:** assignment period rows carry no bucket width, so the tab shows period amounts as stored and compares rated Units/Time with the service-resolved Max Units for the same basis and hours definition. No spreading, rescheduling or leveling runs in the page.
- **Tests at the same code:** `resource-usage.test.ts` 7 tests and `workspace-resources.test.ts` 12 (including the exact resolve POST); full Web 1133 `tsx` + 14 `node --test`, `tsc --noEmit` and esbuild pass; Python `4225 passed, 190 skipped`. `apps/web/e2e/resources-v26.mjs` 13/13 at `35f2283a008c1cb6bb94112e20a291af165eaa5f`, including scenario `05b` (two seeded assignments, API capacity cross-check, exact rows/excess/summary) and the Persian RTL cell.
- **Evidence:** `docs/workspace/evidence/resources-v26-2026-11-14/` (`resources-v26.json` records `testedCodeSha`/`gitHead` = `35f2283a008c1cb6bb94112e20a291af165eaa5f`; 14 screenshots).
- **Remaining limits:** no overtime, timescale/bucket editor, usage profile chart, actual/remaining/forecast spread types or leveling run/apply. RES-005/TRK-001/PRO-002 remain unaudited.

## RES-004 stored usage profile chart — 2026-11-14

- **Implementation (`14e3c16f`):** the Usage tab now shows a **Stored usage profile** chart above the per-period comparison table, with a Day/Week/Month timescale select. `groupResourceUsageBuckets` sums only the stored period rows whose `period_start` falls inside each bucket using exact decimal addition; weeks are UTC Monday–Sunday, months are first-to-last day, and an unparseable stored date stays in its own bucket. A stored amount is never spread across days and no bucket width, spread type or overtime is inferred. Overallocation remains per stored period from the existing comparison, and a bar is flagged only when one of its stored periods is overallocated.
- **Boundary:** the chart counts stored period starts, so two assignments sharing one start appear as one bucket period but still as two assignments in the table row. No scheduler or leveling pass runs in the page.
- **Tests at the same code:** `resource-usage.test.ts` 9 (new bucket-bound and exact weekly/monthly sum cases) and `workspace-resources.test.ts` 13 (new day/week/month DOM case, including unknown-timescale fallback); full Web 1151 `tsx` + 14 `node --test`, `tsc --noEmit` and esbuild pass; Python `4290 passed, 194 skipped`. `apps/web/e2e/resources-v26.mjs` 14/14 at `14e3c16f` on a fresh SQLite preview, with `05b` switching Day→Week→Month and asserting the bucket values, and Persian RTL scenario `12` asserting the option labels and bar facts.
- **Evidence:** `docs/workspace/evidence/res-004-usage-profile-2026-11-14/` (`resources-v26.json` records `testedCodeSha` = `14e3c16f`; day, month, Persian and 1280×720 screenshots).
- **Remaining limits:** no overtime, no stored bucket-width/spread editor, no actual/remaining/forecast spread profiles and no leveling run/apply. RES-005 curves/shifts stay `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL`; TRK-001 lifecycle and PRO-002 remain as recorded.

## RES-005 effective-dated resource rates — 2026-11-14

- **Requirement:** resource prices are effective-dated; the UI must let a writer append a real rate change, preserve the earlier interval, and never compute or guess the rate in force.
- **Implementation (`fe1a306e`):** the Resources Rates tab now appends a change through the existing `p6-resource-rate-write-api.v1` contract (`POST /p6/resource-rates`) with rate, basis, currency, unit, effective interval, `expected_revision` and `close_open_segment`. Closing the stored open-ended interval ends it the day before the new start through the service's append-only supersession. Overlap (`RATE_INTERVALS_OVERLAP`), stale revision (`REVISION_CONFLICT`), permission and conflicting-fact rejections stay server-authoritative and are localized in en/fa. The page reloads the read model after a successful write.
- **Boundary:** no browser-side rate, cost or schedule math. The rate write does not recalculate stored assignment costs and does not advance the project revision; the interval identity plus the service's overlap check are the concurrency guard. Rate selection still uses the explicit `CostBasis` set (`per_unit`, `per_hour`, `per_day`, `lump_sum`) with no separate rate-type dimension.
- **Tests at the same code:** 5 new unit tests in `apps/web/src/workspace-resources.test.ts`; full Web 1138 `tsx` + 14 `node --test`, `tsc --noEmit` and esbuild pass; Python `4225 passed, 190 skipped`. `apps/web/e2e/resources-v26.mjs` 14/14 at `fe1a306e2de5ee3434ec880173aede52d67a2166`: scenario `04b` stores `610` from `2026-11-01`, verifies the API list `480`/`525` closed on `2026-10-31`/`610` open-ended, then asserts an unclosed `700` interval is rejected and not stored; scenario `12` checks the Persian RTL form.
- **Evidence:** `docs/workspace/evidence/resources-v26-2026-11-14/` (`resources-v26.json` records `testedCodeSha`/`gitHead` = `fe1a306e2de5ee3434ec880173aede52d67a2166`; 16 screenshots).
- **Curves and shifts:** `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL`. No curve library, assignment curve selection or bucket width and no resource shift master or shift-aware leveling input exist; the requested contracts and failure codes are in `docs/workspace/architecture/p6-resource-curve-shift-contract-request.md`. TRK-001 and PRO-002 remain unaudited.

## PRO-002 stored period performance — 2026-11-14

- **Shipped (`fd9d52b2`):** the Progress window has a Stored Period Performance section backed by the existing immutable `p6-activity-period-actual-api.v1`. It reads financial periods and stored actuals from the service, validates decimal units/cost locally (at least one required), and posts the exact `contract_version`, `actual_id`, `activity_id`, `period_id`, `actual_units`, `actual_cost`, `unit`, `currency` and `note` payload with the deterministic identity `SPP-<activity>-<period>`.
- **Immutability:** an identical replay is idempotent; a different value for the same activity and period returns `409 IMMUTABLE_ACTIVITY_PERIOD_ACTUAL`; a stale revision returns `409 REVISION_CONFLICT`. The page never overwrites and shows the localized reason in English and Persian.
- **Route fixes:** GET filters now come from the query string (they were previously read only from a body that GET does not have), immutable/revision errors now map to `409` instead of a generic `400`, and a POST rejects a dangling activity or financial period with `404`. `P6ActivityPeriodActualAPI` is mounted in the preview runtime and accepted by `production_composition`; previously the preview built the repository only for the EVM read model, leaving the write route unreachable.
- **Evidence:** `docs/workspace/evidence/progress-history-v26-2026-11-14/` — contract `cubi-progress-history-v26.v2`, `testedCodeSha` `fd9d52b2`, 8/8 scenarios including a real `2026-09` period, `3.125 h / 20.50 USD` read-back after reload, the immutable rejection, viewer read-only and Persian RTL at 1280×720; 7 screenshots, zero exceptions/console errors/API 5xx. 1147 `tsx` + 14 `node --test` web tests, `tsc --noEmit`, production build and 4228 Python tests pass at the same code.
- **Apply Actuals stays `MISSING`:** no data-date, progress estimation/recalculation, activity scope or period reconciliation service exists. No Apply Actuals window or bulk write was invented, and the Progress window performs no schedule or money math.

## PRJ-002 checkout across every write domain — 2026-11-14

- **Shipped (`41aaf874`, code `f2460bee`):** the exclusive project checkout now gates every project-scoped write, not only metadata/status and canonical schedule settings. `src/construction_pm/project_checkout.py` defines one `ProjectCheckoutLease` reader, `ProjectCheckoutConflict` (`PROJECT_CHECKED_OUT_BY_OTHER`) and `require_project_write_ownership`, which runs immediately before each repository write and therefore before any row mutation or history append.
- **Domains:** `P6ActivityMasterAPI` create/update (also the path used by `P6ActivityProgressAPI` and by AI activity creation), `P6WBSMasterAPI` create/update/delete/copy/replace-milestones, `P6RelationshipAPI` create/update/delete and `P6CostAccountAPI` create. The activity, WBS, relationship and cost-account HTTP blocks return `409 PROJECT_CHECKED_OUT_BY_OTHER` with the checkout-conflict message key. The lease holder and an unleased project behave exactly as before.
- **Transactions and backend parity:** the preview lease reads the same SQLite `preview_project` row as the checkout endpoint; the production lease reads `project_lifecycle_projects` through the request-scoped PostgreSQL connection, so the ownership check and the guarded write share one transaction. `production_composition` and both runtimes inject the lease into the HTTP-facing APIs and the AI tool API.
- **Prerequisite defect found and fixed (`e8c4e5bd`):** the Python PostgreSQL schema tuple embedded the `default_cost_account_id` migration inside the `default_duration_type` statement, so `initialize_project_lifecycle_schema` raised `syntax error at or near ")"` on every production boot. The statements are split and a live test now executes the initializer and asserts the deployed columns.
- **Tests at the merge SHA:** `tests/test_project_checkout.py` proves owner vs non-owner behaviour for every write path and asserts no stored row/round trip changes on conflict; route tests cover 409 for activity, WBS, relationship and cost accounts; `tests/test_preview_runtime.py` drives the real preview WSGI app with two real demo sessions (admin checks out, planner rejected on all four domains, nothing persisted, holder still writes); the live PostgreSQL test reads the lease holder, tenant-isolates it and follows check-out/check-in. Post-merge Python 4290 passed / 194 skipped, live PostgreSQL 6 passed, Web 1148 + 14 passed, `tsc` and build clean, E2E 9/9 on a fresh database.
- **Evidence:** `docs/workspace/evidence/project-checkout-write-domains-2026-11-14/` — `summary.md`, targeted and live test logs, E2E log, and the checkout screenshots (`04`, `04b`) captured at `41aaf874`.
- **Remaining:** activity/WBS/relationship/cost conflicts are proven through the preview application rather than the browser; no bulk/import write path exists yet. The enterprise responsibility-structure management window and Apply Actuals remain open.

## PRJ-002 enterprise responsibility structure — 2026-11-14

- **Shipped (`2f39c1de`):** the responsibility structure (OBS in V26 terms) is one tenant-wide hierarchy in `p6_obs_node` instead of a per-project string. `SQLiteP6OBSRepository` and `PostgresP6OBSRepository` store nodes keyed by `(tenant_id, obs_id)` with parent, code, name, manager and sequence; the PostgreSQL table is created by the deployment schema and both store ordering is `sequence_number, obs_id`.
- **Hierarchy authority:** `P6OBSApplicationService.save` validates the node, requires the parent to exist in the same tenant (`OBS_PARENT_NOT_FOUND`) and walks the ancestor chain before the upsert, so a reparenting cycle (`OBS_PARENT_CYCLE`) or self-parent (`OBS_SELF_PARENT`) is rejected without mutating the stored tree.
- **API:** `GET/POST /api/projects/{id}/p6/obs-nodes` (list, single read, save) resolves the authenticated project scope and requires `PROJECT_READ` for reads and `PROJECT_WRITE` for writes; the response is `p6-obs-node-api.v1` with `kind: p6_obs_node`.
- **Project reference:** `ProjectSummary.obs_id` is normalized (`PROJECT_OBS_ID_INVALID`), validated on create and PATCH against the caller's tenant tree (`PROJECT_OBS_NODE_NOT_FOUND`, `PROJECT_OBS_NODE_UNAVAILABLE` without a wiring), preserved when PATCH omits it and cleared with `""`. Preview SQLite (additive migration) and PostgreSQL (`ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`) persist it with `COALESCE`; `infra/project_lifecycle_schema.sql` mirrors the column.
- **UI:** the Project Details General tab selects from the enterprise list loaded when the panel opens; an unseen stored reference stays visible, and the labels now say "Responsibility structure" in en/fa instead of the vendor OBS term.
- **Tests at the merge SHA:** repository ordering/isolation/validation, parent/cycle/valid-reparent, API permissions and scope, HTTP list/get/save and project PATCH set/clear, preview and PostgreSQL persistence, plus the live PostgreSQL assignment round trip. Post-merge Python 4281 passed / 192 skipped, live project-lifecycle PostgreSQL 4 passed, Web 1148 + 14 passed, `tsc` and `npm run build` clean.
- **Browser evidence:** `docs/workspace/evidence/project-details-v26-2026-11-14/` — `testedCodeSha`/`gitHead` `2f39c1de`, 9/9 scenarios, 13 screenshots, zero exceptions/console errors/API failures. Scenario `02` creates `OBS-CIVIL` through the real node API, assigns it, reads it back after a full reload, and the viewer and Persian RTL cells assert the stored reference and the localized label.
- **Remaining:** there is no management window for the hierarchy (nodes are created through the API; no move/delete endpoint), no project rollups by node, and the responsible manager is still a validated project string rather than a node reference.

## SCH-001 typed schedule options and Data Date audit — 2026-11-14

- **Audit (`41d4f267`, test-only):** the Schedule window settings form is real. `apps/web/src/schedule-cycle.ts` renders mode, the stored project calendar, calendar system, Data Date/project start and finish, leveling priority, and one typed control per `ScheduleOptions` field. The schema comes from `src/construction_pm/scheduling_code_reference_api.py::_safe_setting_schema`, which walks the frozen `ScheduleOptions` dataclass and maps enum→select, date→date, bool→checkbox, number→numeric, `resource_list`/`priority_list`→JSON and text otherwise, so the UI cannot drift from the persisted model.
- **Persistence and errors:** `POST /p6/schedule/settings` requires `PROJECT_SCHEDULE` and the checkout lease, validates the stored calendar reference, rejects unknown fields and stale `expected_record_revision` (`REVISION_CONFLICT`), and returns the re-read DTO with the advanced revision. Typed Jalali dates convert to the canonical Gregorian date and fail closed (`INVALID_PROJECT_START`) without changing stored data.
- **Snapshot binding:** `AuthoritativeScheduleInputComposer.compose` re-reads the stored settings and copies `schedule_options` into the input whose canonical payload is hashed. Changing an option therefore changes the published snapshot identity, and the forward pass already treats `data_date` (falling back to project start) as the effective data date.
- **Browser proof at `41d4f267`:** the full-stack suite adds `typed schedule options persist and bind to the next snapshot hash`. It asserts the generated `data-option` controls, saves `maximum_multiple_float_paths=3`, `min_float_to_preserve=2` and `data_date=2026-09-28`, reads the exact values and advanced revision back from the API, republishes and asserts a different hash (baseline `658c9d34…` → `1e772e85…`), then restores the defaults. 98/98 checks, zero exceptions/API 5xx.
- **Tests at the same code:** Web 1151 + 14, Python 4290 passed / 194 skipped; existing server tests cover unknown fields, stale revision, permission denial, checkout lease, numeric/date validation and Data Date CPM semantics.
- **Evidence:** `docs/workspace/evidence/sch-001-schedule-options-2026-11-14/` (`evidence.json` `git_head` = `41d4f267c24dde9b492adaaab86f54d8eda0f2fd`).

## SCH-002 CPM, float, critical path and multiple float paths — 2026-11-14

- **Audit (`5d923063`):** CPM is entirely server-side. `src/construction_pm/scheduling/forward_pass.py` drives the forward pass (relationship types, lags with calendars, constraints, Data Date falling back to project start); `scheduling/schedule.py` runs the backward pass and computes early/late dates, total and free float, criticality (float threshold, longest path, open-ended) and `_multiple_float_paths`; `backend_p0/authoritative_schedule_query.py` returns start/finish, duration, early/late dates, total/free float, `is_critical`, `float_path` and `float_path_order`.
- **Read model:** `apps/web/src/workspace-model.ts::applyAuthoritativeScheduleFacts` maps the authoritative values into the registered `activity.*` cells, so no browser code recomputes CPM or float.
- **Change:** `apps/web/src/schedule-cycle.ts::renderScheduleRunResult` now renders **Float path** and **Float path order** columns (`data-float-path`, `data-float-path-order`, en/fa labels) whenever any authoritative row carries a path; missing values render as an em dash. The float columns stay conditional on the response, so a schedule without paths is unchanged.
- **Browser proof:** `apps/web/e2e/preview-e2e.mjs` records `multiple float paths are calculated and rendered per activity`: enable `multiple_float_paths_enabled`, set `maximum_multiple_float_paths=2` through the revision-guarded settings POST (revision 5), publish, query the authoritative schedule, assert both headers, and assert a positive integer path/order on every row that carries one — `A100 p1 o1`, `A110 p1 o2`, `A120 p1 o3`, `A130 p1 o4`, `A145 p2 o1` — then restore defaults. Full run at `5d923063`: 99/99 checks, 53 screenshots, zero uncaught exceptions and zero API 5xx.
- **Tests at the same code:** `tests/scheduling/test_forward_pass.py`, `test_schedule.py`, `test_multi_project_float.py`, `test_oos_policy.py`, `test_out_of_sequence.py`, `test_authoritative_schedule.py` and `test_authoritative_schedule_batch.py`; Python 4290 passed / 194 skipped, Web 1151 + 14, `tsc` and esbuild clean.
- **Evidence:** `docs/workspace/evidence/sch-002-float-paths-2026-11-14/` (`evidence.json` `git_head` = `5d9230636be2c086d987f4b58b50ced1bf3ae705`).
- **Remaining:** external Version 26 fixture comparison is `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL` (no licensed V26 schedule export in the repository). The run-results table exposes only start/finish/duration/total float/float path/critical; early/late dates, free float and diagnostics remain in the register query and grid columns.


## CUBI Enterprise — mandatory P6 EPPM Help V26 HTML baseline and Task Center UI slice — 2026-11-15

- **Owner decision:** all CUBI Enterprise design, implementation, QA and acceptance must be checked against the Oracle P6 EPPM Help V26 English HTML baseline and the separate P6 Team Member Help V26 HTML set. The V26 “Working with P6” HTML topic lists seven main sections: Dashboards, Portfolios, Projects, Resources, Approvals, Reports and Administration. Canonical matrix: `docs/enterprise/CUBI_ENTERPRISE_PRIMAVERA_P6_EPPM_HELP_PARITY_V26.md`.
- **Normative HTML sources:** https://docs.oracle.com/cd/G48897_01/p6help/en/toc.htm ; https://docs.oracle.com/cd/G48897_01/team_member/web/help/en/toc.htm ; V26 User Guides / All Guides indexes. Version 26 is the documented baseline as of 2026-11-15; update only after an explicit reviewed version delta.
- **Interpretation:** match concepts, workflows, navigation families, role/security behavior and acceptance evidence in CUBI-native language/design; do not copy Oracle branding/assets. Do not claim generic Enterprise Tasks are P6 schedule Activities or Team Member timesheets. Engineering remains the authoritative schedule/CPM/activity/WBS/resource/cost/progress/EVM source.
- **Implementation branch:** `feat/cubi-enterprise-task-center-v1`, based on verified task API PR #1951 head `3ab5624b4921e667db089856b084f7ee8a580c42`.
- **UI slice added:** isolated `/enterprise` route, authenticated Task Center, personal/project scope selection, all/active/due-today/overdue/blocked/completed filters, title/description search, create form with priority and due date, revision-checked start/complete/reopen actions, same-origin CSRF and idempotency key; responsive and Persian RTL styles; local preview and Vercel shell routing.
- **Tests added:** `apps/web/src/enterprise-task-center.test.ts` covers active status, overdue exclusion and case-insensitive search. Exact-head CI and browser E2E remain pending; this is not merged or deployed.

- **Enterprise environment security hardening:** task application service now fails closed unless server-side session roles include `enterprise_viewer`, `enterprise_user` or `enterprise_admin`; write actions require `enterprise_user` or `enterprise_admin`. Project-only roles alone no longer expose the Enterprise task API. Production PostgreSQL integration includes an authenticated project admin without an Enterprise grant and asserts 403 on both list and create. Organization & Access must own role assignment/revocation/audit later; until then trusted identity provisioning is the only grant path.
- **Explicit gaps:** team assignment remains server-disabled until Organization & Access membership persistence; Engineering references remain server-disabled until authorized revision-pinned resolver; P6 Team Member schedule Activity status, steps, timesheets and approvals are not in this generic task UI. Full EPPM Help V26 feature-family parity remains a tracked staged program, not completed.
- **No merge/deploy.** Engineering priority and authoritative data ownership remain unchanged.

## SCH-003 constraints, open ends and relationship lag calendar — 2026-11-14

- **Audit (`e81d3bb0`):** `constraint_master_repository.py` persisted revision-scoped constraint rows, and the authoritative input composer plus forward pass already applied them, but there was no HTTP route, no delete, and no typed UI. `ConstraintMaster.validate()` also accepted `datetime` precision because `datetime` subclasses `date`.
- **Backend:** the repository now rejects datetime with `INVALID_CONSTRAINT_DATE`; both SQLite and PostgreSQL `delete` implementations delete by tenant/project/revision/id/record_revision and raise `CONSTRAINT_NOT_FOUND` or `REVISION_CONFLICT`. The new `src/construction_pm/p6_constraint_api.py` implements `p6-constraint-api.v1` (`kind: p6_constraint`) for create/get/list/update/delete behind `PROJECT_READ`/`PROJECT_WRITE` and the checkout lease, with revision guards, activity existence (`ACTIVITY_NOT_FOUND_OR_SCOPE_MISMATCH`), Level of Effort/WBS Summary rejection (`CONSTRAINT_ACTIVITY_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED:<type>`) and duplicate `(activity,type)` rejection (`DUPLICATE_CONSTRAINT_TYPE`). Routes are `GET/POST /api/projects/{id}/p6/constraints` and `GET/PATCH/DELETE …/constraints/{constraint_id}` with 404/409/400/403 mappings; preview and production runtimes build the API from the real repository.
- **UI/read model:** `apps/web/src/workspace-constraints.ts` is the typed client (six values, en/fa labels, strict response/payload validation, delete with `expected_revision`). The Activity Details Status tab lists stored constraints for the selected activity with revision and a Remove action only for writers, and renders a write-only form with exactly six type options plus a date input. Saving reloads from the API; the pane states that constraints are inputs and that dates, float and criticality are calculated only after the schedule is published and run.
- **Browser proof:** `apps/web/e2e/preview-e2e.mjs` selects `A140`, asserts an empty list and six options, saves `START_NO_EARLIER_THAN 2026-10-20`, reads the exact record at revision 1 from `GET /p6/constraints`, publishes and runs the schedule to `A140` start `2026-10-20` / finish `2026-11-03` / total float `0` / critical `Yes`, screenshots `07h-constraint-editor.png`, deletes via the UI, asserts the list is empty and the rerun starts before `2026-10-20`. Full run at `e81d3bb0`: 100/100 checks, 54 screenshots, zero exceptions/API 5xx.
- **Tests at the same code:** `tests/test_p6_constraint_api.py`, constraint repository datetime/delete cases, the constraint HTTP block, `workspace-constraints.test.ts` and the activity-details constraint DOM test; Python `4319 passed / 203 skipped`, Web `1162 + 15`, `tsc` and esbuild clean.
- **Evidence:** `docs/workspace/evidence/sch-003-constraints-2026-11-14/` (`evidence.json` `git_head` = `e81d3bb09773eaad4a749204117d507ba59161b1`).
- **Remaining:** per-relationship lag-calendar assignment has repository and composer support (`relationship_lag_calendar_assignment`, `save_relationship_lag`) but no HTTP/UI editor; the project-wide `relationship_lag_calendar` option is typed, stored and consumed. External V26 fixture comparison is `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL`; open-ended criticality is a core option rather than a separate control, and leveling interactions remain SCH-005.

## SCH-004 Schedule Check and diagnostics — 2026-11-14

- **Audit (`8948bff4`):** `src/construction_pm/scheduling/diagnostics.py` already raised `ScheduleInfeasibilityError` and recorded negative-float diagnostics, and `backend_p0/authoritative_schedule_query.py` returned them, but there was no rule catalogue, no counts and no UI. The six conditions were only implicit: open ends in the relationship topology, constraints in the composer input and negative lag in the relationship lags.
- **Core:** new `src/construction_pm/scheduling/schedule_checks.py` defines exactly six rules — `NEGATIVE_FLOAT`, `OPEN_START`, `OPEN_FINISH`, `ACTIVITY_CONSTRAINT`, `MANDATORY_CONSTRAINT`, `NEGATIVE_LAG` — with fixed severities. `evaluate_schedule_checks(source, date_result=…/time_result=…)` derives open ends while excluding WBS Summary and Level of Effort, splits mandatory from non-mandatory constraints, reports negative lags and negative float, and sorts deterministically by catalogue order then identity. `build_schedule_check_payload` emits `p6-schedule-check.v1` (`kind: schedule_check`) with scope, snapshot identity, total/severity counts, a rules array including zero-count rules and ordered findings. `ScheduleEvaluationResult` carries the findings and the date/time FACT and EXPLANATION responses include the block; FILTER is unchanged.
- **UI:** `apps/web/src/schedule-cycle.ts` strictly parses the block (absent or older → `null`, malformed → throw) and renders counts, one row per rule including zero counts, findings and a focus button. `mountRunPanel` selects and scrolls the matching activity grid row. English and Persian labels cover the title, counts, findings and all six rules. The check is rendered in the Schedule window run-results panel, not as a separate docked window.
- **Browser proof:** `apps/web/e2e/preview-e2e.mjs` records `schedule check lists conditions, counts and drills through to an activity`. With the `A140` constraint present, the panel reports an `ACTIVITY_CONSTRAINT` finding for `A140` and the total equals the sum of the counts. After deleting the constraint and rerunning, the six rules read `NEGATIVE_FLOAT 0 / OPEN_START 1 / OPEN_FINISH 2 / ACTIVITY_CONSTRAINT 0 / MANDATORY_CONSTRAINT 0 / NEGATIVE_LAG 0`, total `3`, open start `A100`, open finish `A210`; clicking the `A100` focus button selects that grid row. Full run at `8948bff4`: 101/101 checks, 55 screenshots, zero uncaught exceptions and zero API 5xx. Screenshot `07i-schedule-check.png` is captured but the schedule panels are clipped in the P6 shell preview, so the DOM assertions are the authoritative proof.
- **Tests at the same code:** new `tests/scheduling/test_schedule_checks.py` (6 tests: open-end exclusions, constraint classification, negative lag/float, time-aware negative float, zero-count payload consistency, stable empty input), extended `tests/test_authoritative_schedule_query.py` (contract, counts, per-rule counts and finding order), and 3 new `apps/web/src/schedule-cycle.test.ts` cases (parse/scope, absent vs malformed, rendered counts/rules/findings/focus). Python `4329 passed / 204 skipped`, Web `1165 + 15`, `tsc` and esbuild clean.
- **Evidence:** `docs/workspace/evidence/sch-004-schedule-check-2026-11-14/` (`evidence.json` `git_head` = `8948bff4dec014dcab18fcdc859b4e15ffeaf3b7`, with the `schedule_check` block).
- **Remaining:** the rule catalogue is bounded to the six conditions the core can evaluate today; the external V26 rule-catalogue comparison is `BLOCKED_EXTERNAL` (no licensed V26 schedule export or rule fixture). There is no separate docked Schedule Check window, and negative float remains a WARNING in the check layer while genuine infeasibility still raises `ScheduleInfeasibilityError`.

## 2026-11-19 — P0-M intra-project parallel scheduling core (backward/float pipeline)

- Scope: Scheduling calculation core only (time-aware CPM on the canonical engine). No frontend, API surface, page load, deployment or Vercel work. No scheduling rule, calendar, relationship, constraint, float/critical-path definition, decision order, validation message or output field changed.
- Measurement first: sequential phase profiling at 1k/5k/25k/100k showed forward 26/133/744/3,188 ms, backward 89/447/2,351/9,442 ms, float 108/544/2,802/11,207 ms, assembly 10/54/287/1,152 ms, hashing 4/21/109/460 ms; the float phase is 44–46 % of every run and is the only phase whose inputs are complete and whose activities are mutually independent.
- Wavefront decision (measured, not assumed): the mandated benchmark DAG is a spine with forward shortcuts (`depth == size`, `width_max == 1` at every size). One pool barrier per level costs 3,225 ms at 25k (measured 0.129 ms round trip, 3 children) against a 722 ms serial forward pass, i.e. 4.47× worse before any calculation (chain 5.76×, mixed 3.11×). Forward and backward stay sequential. On `fan`/`wide` shapes (depth 3/1, width 24,998/25,000) the measured barrier overhead is 0.39/0.13 ms, so a wavefront could pay there; that architecture was measured and recorded but not built and is not claimed.
- Change: `time_backward_pass(..., on_late=...)` reports each final late record; `_time_float_for_activity` accepts the activity's own late record (it reads no other late entry); `_FloatPipeline` batches `(id, late)` pairs (~108 B each, ≈32 batches per project) to fork children running the unchanged engine function while the parent keeps walking back; `finish()` merges in sorted id order. `_float_pipeline` requires a safe start method, more than one granted slot, ≥256 activities, no active pool and a pool that really starts; it holds `_FLOAT_POOL_GUARD`, keeps one slot for the parent (`granted − 1` children), and releases on every path. All other states run the previous code path verbatim, including the post-hoc parallel float pass. `float_workers` is validated before any work because the pipeline path no longer calls `calculate_time_floats`.
- Result (whole-run medians, same host/fixtures/seed; one fresh process per configuration, warm-up, 5 repeats at 1k/5k/10k and 3 at 25k/50k/100k): 1,000 225.4 → 167.8 → 152.5 ms; 5,000 1,138.8 → 780.0 → 669.3 ms; 10,000 2,281.5 → 1,546.2 → 1,371.1 ms; 25,000 5,825.9 → 4,250.2 → 3,583.8 ms; 50,000 11,990.0 → 8,930.2 → 7,608.5 ms; 100,000 24,202.1 → 18,051.6 → 15,012.9 ms for 1/2/4 slots (1.48×/1.70×/1.66×/1.63×/1.58×/1.61× at 4 slots). The post-hoc P0-H/P0-J path measures 157.4/771.1/1,555.6/3,978.6/8,246.8/16,447.0 ms at 4 slots, so the pipeline is 3–13 % faster and uses fewer child processes. Total CPU utilisation is 1.77–1.88 of 4 CPUs; child-worker utilisation 0.26–0.30 because the parent's serial pass is the bottleneck. Serialization + submission is 0.25–2.05 ms per project (0.02 % of the 9.19 s gain at 100k).
- Correctness: 20 new tests compare 1/2/4 (and 8) slots against the sequential Oracle on payload, mapping order, project finish and every float field, across FS/SS/FF/SF with ±lags, multiple calendars and exception layers, per-activity calendar override, DST, constraints, actual start with out-of-sequence SS, all six fixture shapes, a bounded Free Float window and unconnected activities; fallbacks (threaded process, below-threshold, one granted slot, active pool, failure with guard release) are asserted. Every canonical hash equals baseline `9408552b` for time dag 1k/5k/25k/100k and time mixed 25k, and equals itself across 1/2/4 slots and both modes. Full suite on the tested tree: 5,292 passed, 1 skipped, 0 failed (baseline 5,272 + 20 new tests).
- Memory: pipeline batch buffers are bounded by the batch size (~110 kB in flight); parent peak RSS at 100k is 670 MB (+18 % over sequential) and summed child RSS is 1,450 MB, which overcounts copy-on-write pages. No uncontrolled growth.
- Limits recorded: the attainable ceiling is set by the serial parent pass (≈1.79–1.85× at 4 slots); 2 slots leave one child that becomes the bottleneck at ≥25k; below 256 activities the pipeline declines; `spawn` stays off by default; result unpickling adds ≈5 % at 100k; wide-graph wavefront parallelism is measured but not implemented.
- Evidence: `docs/workspace/evidence/p0m-intra-project-pipeline-2026-11-18/` (summary, comparison, raw and re-measured matrix logs, phase profiles, wavefront cost model, before/after hashes, full-suite log, source hashes, reproduction scripts); feasibility record §19. Branch `p0m-intra-project-pipeline`; squash-merged into `main` as `2e1e9b2a131e3bc1001299dda8c383f9f678d980` (PR #2057, 2026-11-19T05:26:13Z). Required CI passed (only infrastructure-only Vercel deployments failed). Post-merge on `main` @`2e1e9b2a`: 5,292 passed / 1 skipped and identical canonical hashes.

## 2026-11-19 — P0-N global orchestration, process placement and adaptive cache

- Scope: scheduling calculation core only. No scheduling rule, calendar, relationship, constraint, float/critical-path definition, decision order, validation message, output field or API surface changed. Placement may change elapsed time only.
- Audit (recorded in feasibility §20.2): `SchedulingJobExecutor` runs each job on a daemon thread plus a `_LeaseHeartbeat` thread, so `_fork_is_safe()` is false in the real loop and the P0-M pipeline declined there; `evaluate_schedule_snapshot(..., float_execution=None)` also left `float_workers=1`. Measured: four 5k projects as threads 5,845 ms vs 5,666 ms sequential (GIL), as fork processes 1,517 ms (3.7×), spawn 1,767 ms. The executor has no application entry point today; the worker harness is the production-shaped driver.
- Design: `GlobalWorkerScheduler` runs one process per project from a single-threaded dispatch loop and holds the job's slots from the one `GlobalExecutionBudget` for the whole run; lease renewal is synchronous, so the job process can fork float children safely. `SlotAllocationPolicy` (small-first, one slot for ≤1,500 activities, desired `ceil(activities/1250)`, fair share `free // waiting`, work conservation for a lone project, aging) replaces both the "first job takes everything" and "cap the number of jobs" heuristics. `make_slot_admission(slots)` bounds the child's float pool to `slots - 1`. `FloatExecutionPolicy.allow_nested_fork` is the explicit opt-in the scheduler grants. `ScheduleResultCache` is a tenant-isolated weighted LRU with `adapt(active_jobs, active_tenants, available_bytes)`; `CacheKey` covers tenant, project, calculation identity, engine version, optional snapshot hash and schema.
- Executor: `execution_mode="process"` (new) claims → cache → reserves slots → forked child or exact inline fallback → reap → persist, renewing leases from the dispatch loop; `thread` remains the default and its code path is unchanged. `calculate_with_slots(job, slots)` is optional; without it the process runs the exact `calculate`.
- Result (4 CPUs, budget 4, 3 repeats, medians): 1/2/4/8/10 large projects 993/1,437/1,609/3,352/3,933 ms; 10+5 small 4,240 ms; 10+20 small 5,021 ms; 10 staggered 3,960 ms. Sequential for the 30-project mix 17,287 ms, for the ten large 13,397 ms → 3.41× and 3.44×. Single project 1,398 → 993 ms; float pass inside it 1,120 → 671 ms (1.67×, P0-M reproduced). Worst small-project queue wait 88 ms with ten large queued, 920 ms with twenty smalls competing. Capacity sweep 1/2/4/8/16/32 slots: 13,689/6,952/3,955/3,682/3,671/4,180 ms at 24.7/48.5/85.5/92.8/93.1/95.2 % CPU; throughput saturates at 8 slots and 32 oversubscribes (−12 % against 16). Thread baseline on four large 5,093 ms (25.1 % CPU) vs 1,609 ms (3.17×). Executor end to end (4 large + 4 small) 1,618 ms, 8/8 process starts, `peak_slots=4`, 8/8 persisted hashes equal. Cache cold 3,939 ms / warm 0.2 ms (10/10 hits, 3,618 bytes). Two-CPU affinity cap: 7,416 ms at 1.85 of 2 available cores. Soak 8×2 large: parent RSS 93.0 MB flat, zero slots leaked.
- Correctness: 47 new tests (`test_worker_scheduler.py` 20, `test_schedule_result_cache.py` 13, `test_scheduling_job_executor_process.py` 11, `test_p0n_postgres_adapter.py` 3 on PostgreSQL) cover policy arithmetic, real-process equality across shapes, `peak_slots=4` for a lone project, cache hit with zero process starts, engine-version key miss, cancellation releasing every slot, lease renewal, spawn fallback to the exact inline run, two schedulers sharing one budget, staggered arrivals, cache concurrency/fair share/adaptive shrink, and the PostgreSQL adapter with tenant isolation. Every canonical hash equals the P0-M baseline (`3d2f1f8d` time dag 5k and the 1k/25k/100k/mixed set) in every scenario and both adapters. Local suite: 5,161 passed, 132 skipped, 0 failed; `tests/scheduling` 1,656 passed.
- Limits: forward/backward stay serial per project (44–46 % float share bounds intra-project speedup at 1.42–1.67×); a two-slot grant buys one float child the pipeline does not use, so it behaves like one slot (no wall-time loss, but visible idle CPU when only two projects run); `thread` stays the default so the mode switch remains an application-wiring decision; `spawn` stays opt-in; intermediate float context is not cached; wide-graph wavefront parallelism is measured and still not implemented.
- Evidence: `docs/workspace/evidence/p0n-global-orchestration-2026-11-19/` (raw full-matrix JSON and logs, affinity, executor end-to-end, scheduling and PostgreSQL suite logs, environment and source hashes, reproduction commands); feasibility record §20. Branch `p0n-global-scheduler`.

- Merge: PR #2060 squash-merged into `main` as `3b30cec0baf540580129d61286e1d696a67a24ff` (2026-11-19); required CI passed (only infrastructure-only Vercel deployments failed). Post-merge on `main` @`3b30cec0`: 5,161 passed / 132 skipped, identical canonical hashes (`3d2f1f8d` / `bdb8a046` / `84864c94` / `e1c4396f` / `7c6aa1b2`).

## 2026-11-19 — Stability follow-up: shared SQLite snapshot reads

- Trigger: the P0-N post-merge suite on `main` @`d54cf847` (docs-only) reported `1 failed, 5160 passed, 132 skipped` in `tests/scheduling/test_scheduling_job_authoritative_path.py` (`claimed=3, succeeded=1, failed=2`, `process_starts=0`). The same test had failed once before P0-N and was initially recorded as flaky.
- Root cause (reproduced, not inferred): `SQLiteScheduleInputSnapshotRepository` was shared by executor worker threads over one `sqlite3` connection. `check_same_thread=False` allows the calls but not interleaved statement execution; a concurrent `execute` resets another thread's statement, so reads returned `None`/foreign rows and the provider raised `SCHEDULE_INPUT_SNAPSHOT_NOT_FOUND`. An eight-thread probe produced `INVALID_PROJECT_REVISION`, `INVALID_SNAPSHOT_TIMESTAMP`, `REVISION_CONFLICT`, `INVALID_SNAPSHOT_HASH` and `sqlite3.InterfaceError`. Failure rate on the test file was 2/10 isolated runs.
- Change: every connection access in the SQLite repository (`save`, `get`, `list`, `migrate_calculation_identity`) is serialised by a per-repository `RLock` (reentrant because migration calls `get`). No query, revision rule, persisted content or calculation path changed. New regression test `test_sqlite_shared_connection_is_safe_for_concurrent_readers` fails 3/3 with the lock bypassed and passes with it.
- Verification: previously flaky file 20/20 consecutive runs green (and 10/10 again post-merge); full suite with Local and PostgreSQL adapters 5,340 passed / 1 skipped / 0 failed; scheduling+repository subset 1,684 passed; post-fix full benchmark 24/24 scenario repeats, 6/6 capacity points, 8/8 executor jobs, 10/10 cache hits equal the sequential hash, scenario E 3,899 ms, sweep 13,605/6,939/3,926/3,674/3,531/4,124 ms, soak RSS flat at 94.3 MB.
- Evidence: `docs/workspace/evidence/p0n-shared-sqlite-thread-safety-2026-11-19/`; feasibility record §20.10. Branch `fix/sqlite-snapshot-thread-safety`.

## 2026-11-20 — P0-N production job integration

- Trigger: the P0-N engine was reachable only from tests. `POST /api/v1/schedule/query` calculates synchronously in the request, and the durable job lifecycle had no persisted run input, no worker endpoint and no HTTP submission path. Acceptance required one real API request to submit a job, a worker to execute it on the optimized engine, and the same API to return status and result.
- Change: `SchedulingJob.run_parameters` (immutable sorted string pairs: snapshot id, project revision, submitter roles, workload hints) persisted with the job; PostgreSQL `run_parameters_json` column added with `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`; a different parameter set under one fingerprint is `SCHEDULING_JOB_PARAMETER_CONFLICT`. `SchedulingEngineJobRunner` runs the existing `SchedulingJobExecutor` under the one `GlobalExecutionBudget`, `SlotAllocationPolicy` and `ScheduleResultCache`, rebuilds the submitter's authorization scope from the persisted roles, fails closed without a stored snapshot, and recovers expired leases at the start of a tick. `SchedulingJobAPI` adds submit/status/result/cancel plus a token-guarded tick; with a snapshot repository the calculation identity is the snapshot's own identity. Routes: `POST/GET /api/v1/schedule/projects/{project_id}/jobs[...]`, `POST .../cancel`, `POST /api/v1/schedule/worker/tick`, `GET /api/v1/schedule/worker/status` (`X-Worker-Token`). Composition gains a job repository, optional runner, execution mode and worker token (env fallback). `thread` stays the default; `process` requires a `provider_factory` so a forked child builds its own repositories, and composition refuses process mode without an explicit runner. No scheduling rule, calendar, relationship, constraint, float definition, decision order or output field changed; `POST /api/v1/schedule/query` is unchanged.
- Defect found and fixed: the WSGI adapter's status map lacked `202 Accepted` (and `405`), so an accepted submission was labelled `202 Internal Server Error`; the code was already 202, only the reason phrase was wrong.
- Verification on tested tree `1b19e617` (tree `ac863807`): new files + PostgreSQL 37 passed; scheduling/composition/HTTP regression subset 1,827 passed; full suite 5,186 passed / 132 skipped / 0 failed. Proven: stored hash equals a direct provider execution; real child process (`process_starts == 1`) with the same hash; tenant-scoped cache hit on the repeat run with no second process start; timeout recorded as `SCHEDULING_JOB_TIMEOUT`; expired lease recovered and executed exactly once; two concurrent workers execute a job once; tenant/project isolation; worker token enforcement; `/api/v1/schedule/query` contract unchanged.
- Evidence: `docs/workspace/evidence/p0n-production-job-integration-2026-11-20/`; feasibility record §21. Branch `feat/p0n-production-job-integration`.
- Merge: PR #2066 squash-merged into `main` as `666a30e7b98dac3c48687058e85a3f1a313523fe` (2026-11-20, issue #2065); required CI passed (only infrastructure-only Vercel deployments failed). Post-merge on `main` @`666a30e7`: 5,186 passed / 132 skipped / 0 failed.
- Limit: the deployed `ProductionRuntime` still raises `SCHEDULE_QUERY_NOT_DEPLOYED` from `_calendar_registry_not_wired`, so it cannot compose a provider that resolves calendars; enabling the routes there needs the calendar source, not a change to this integration.

## 2026-11-20 — P0 Slice 1: single canonical shell and reachable Enterprise surface — Issue #2069 (PR #2070, open)

- **Scope:** P0 of the CUBI site/product integration mission. Routing, session-gated environment selection, one shell for both workspaces, E2E harness. No calculation core, `production_runtime.py` or `schema.py` change; no parallel database and no new temporary storage. Base SHA `ae6e72e48be624c841b579fb9a54dcc36890b99d` (= `origin/main` at branch time).
- **Defect fixed (real, reproduced):** `GET /enterprise` returned 404 in the production WSGI runtime, and `apps/web/api/shell.py` pinned `PATH_INFO=/app` for every workspace route, so Enterprise was unreachable even for an authorised role. `StaticSite` also returned early for single-segment paths, so `/environments/` never resolved its directory index.
- **Change:** `production_web` adds `WORKSPACE_PATHS {/app, /enterprise}`, dispatches both to the app handler, maps `/enterprise` and `/enterprise/index.html` to the same `app.html`, and resolves directory indexes for single-segment static paths. `api/shell.py` derives the workspace from `__path` (query or `PATH_INFO`), defaulting to `/app`; unknown values stay on `/app`. Root and `apps/web` `vercel.json` rewrite `/app` and `/enterprise` to the shell with the matching `__path`. New `apps/web/public/environments/index.html` is a session-aware selector: `401` redirects to `/login?next=/environments/`, Engineering links to `/app?view=live`, Enterprise is a link only when the session roles include `enterprise_viewer`/`user`/`admin` and otherwise renders disabled with "Not enabled for this account". `login.html` now defaults its post-login destination to `/environments/`. `assemble-pages-site.mjs` disallows `/environments` and `/login`, asserts the selector ships, is `noindex`, and is absent from the sitemap.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_production_web_app.py` + `tests/test_vercel_entrypoints.py` **37 passed** locally on PostgreSQL-backed fixtures; three new production-web cases (Enterprise gated like `/app`, Enterprise serves the one canonical shell, static mapping) and the per-route shell resolution case. `apps/web` `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `assemble-pages-site.mjs` produced 16 validated HTML files.

Workspace-route evidence is therefore local production-runtime E2E; a deployed workspace preview remains **BLOCKED_EXTERNAL** until those projects build.

## 2026-11-24 — P0 Engineering Phase D: rebindable shortcuts, conflict rejection, browser-reserved Ctrl+R — Issue #2138

- **Scope:** Engineering shortcut dialog and key handler only. No schema, migration, RLS, importer, project record, identity or session change.
- **Rebinding (PR #2146, branch `feat/engineering-shortcut-rebinding`):** each row gains **Change**, an overridden row gains **Reset**, and the footer gains **Reset all to defaults** (only when deviations exist). Change starts a capture that owns the keyboard: Escape cancels quietly, a bare letter or bare modifier is rejected with the reason (a plain letter would hijack typing), and a chord already bound elsewhere is rejected naming the conflicting command. The accepted chord is reported to the workspace, which persists it per signed-in user and re-renders/reopens the dialog so the catalogue and the live handler always agree.
- **Persistence and validation:** `workspace-shortcut-preferences.ts` stores only deviations under `cubi:workspace-shortcut-overrides:<user>`; on read, unknown ids, empty keys, malformed chords, non-object payloads and bad JSON are dropped, and overrides apply only to ids still present in the current catalogue — a stale or hand-edited value can never bind a key to a command that no longer exists.
- **Lifecycle safety:** a re-render cancels any in-flight capture (the dialog DOM is replaced), and a capture blocks the menu Escape handler so Escape cancels the capture without pulling focus out of the dialog.
- **Ctrl+R:** V26 Help lists Ctrl+R beside F5. The catalogue now records `shortcut.refresh-ctrl-r` as explicitly unavailable with the browser-reload reason and the F5 substitute, so the shell neither hijacks reload nor hides the documented binding.
- **Tests:** new `workspace-shortcut-preferences.test.ts` (7 cases: per-user round-trip and clearing, rejection of stale/malformed payloads, chord capture rules, apply/without, conflict normalization) plus 4 shell cases (dialog controls and browser-reserved reason, capture rejecting typing/conflicts then accepting, Reset/Reset all payloads, re-render cancelling capture). `apps/web` `npm test` **1,344 passed / 0 failed** + preview-server **16 passed**; `npm run typecheck` and `npm run build` exit 0.
- **Per-command official Help map (`workspace-command-help.ts`):** every toolbar command and every shortcut command now resolves to a traceability row in `docs/traceability/engineering-v26.json` and to that row's recorded P6 V26 topic; the shortcuts dialog renders a Help link per mapped row (new tab, `noopener`, labelled with the topic title). Commands P6 does not document (assistant, comments, notes, proposals, forms, backup, refresh, Find, fullscreen, line numbers, window close, Excel/web publish, user preferences) are listed with an explicit reason and get no link. Coverage test walks the toolbar registry plus all shortcut commands, asserts row existence, that every cited URL is `docs.oracle.com`, and that a command is never both mapped and unmapped.
- **Tests (Help map increment):** `apps/web` `npm test` **1,351 passed / 0 failed** + preview-server **16 passed**; typecheck and build exit 0.
- **Status:** PR #2146 OPEN (CI on head `b46bb00b`); PR #2145 merged as `55ec8e75`. Phase D is complete. Next bounded work: Phase E — the 21 navigation groups against canonical PostgreSQL.

## 2026-11-24 — P0 Engineering Phase E (third slice): production chain audit, issues route, durable calendar registry — Issue #2138

Composition asymmetry is the root cause: the preview runtime passes 57 seam kwargs, production passed 23, and every omitted kwarg turned an implemented branch into a dead one.
- **Slice 3a — issues route (PR #2150, MERGED as `e76a38a44`):** `ProductionRuntime` built `P6IssueAPI` and never passed `p6_issue_api`, so every `/api/projects/:id/p6/issues*` call answered `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` while threshold monitoring could still write issues through the same API. One kwarg restores the route for the Engineering Issues window. Live PostgreSQL test: empty register → create → re-read in a new request plus direct `p6_issue` row → duplicate 409 → guarded edit to revision 2 → stale revision 409 with unchanged data → durable `p6_issue_event` history → viewer read 200 / write 403 → foreign tenant 403 → delete at revision 2 with zero rows and the trail ending in `deleted`. Removing the kwarg makes the test fail with `404 == 200`.
- **Slice 3b — calendar registry (PR #2151, branch `feat/engineering-calendar-registry`, head `c38391877d7ad06db385936e0838df4c26e78171`):** production passed `_calendar_registry_not_wired`, so `POST /api/v1/schedule/query` answered a generic **503** and every calendar route was gated. New `deployment/calendar_registry.py` reads the durable masters, snapshots, work-hour rules and exception layers through the PostgreSQL repositories and fails closed with `CalendarRegistryError`; the runtime now builds the calendar repositories, `P6CalendarAPI` and `P6CalendarReadAPI` and passes the real factory. **No migration was added:** step 14 already creates the six calendar tables and step 16 hardens them — verified by applying the registry to a fresh schema (17 steps / 98 tables with the calendar tables present). Live test: catalog 200, master create, durable row, registry resolves the calendar to a resolver, schedule query now fails at the evaluator with `SCHEDULE_INPUT_SNAPSHOT_ID_REQUIRED` instead of 503, viewer read 200 / write 403, delete clears the row. Reverting the wiring restores `404 == 200`.
- **Tests:** full Python suite with isolated PostgreSQL 14 — slice 3a **5,560 passed / 20 skipped**, slice 3b **5,561 passed / 20 skipped / 15 subtests**. No web change, so `npm test` / typecheck / build are unchanged from #2149 (1,351 + 16, exit 0).
- **Not changed:** no production or Supabase database was touched, no migration was applied outside the isolated local engine, and CUBI-REF-001/002/003 and CUBI-DAM-001 were not read, written or deleted.
- **Remaining chain gaps (ranked):** (1) `schedule_facts_provider` is still not passed to the workspace read, so total float / free float / critical path cells stay empty — it should be wired together with snapshot publication so the pair is testable; (2) `p6_schedule_snapshot_api` and the scheduling-job repository are still not passed, so snapshot publication and rescheduling are inert; (3) resources, assignments, budgets, costs, expenses, earned value, baselines, reports and closeout have no production route or table; (4) OBS has no optimistic-concurrency guard while every other master does; (5) EPS is absent by design — confirm before adding; (6) live row-level verification of the four Showcase projects still needs a disposable authenticated identity or read-only DSN.
- **Status:** PR #2150 MERGED; PR #2151 OPEN, merge only after every CI job is green. Next executive action: wire the schedule facts provider together with snapshot publication so float and critical path become observable, then close the resource/cost/report/closeout gaps.

## 2026-11-24 — P0 Engineering Phase E (fourth slice): published snapshots project float and critical path in production — Issue #2138

- **main SHA at slice start:** `57bcc3e8e402edf92564d131df643c5f6ef78aa1` (verified against `origin/main` before editing).
- **Correction carried forward:** the previous entry recorded PR #2151 as OPEN. It is **MERGED** as `57bcc3e8e402edf92564d131df643c5f6ef78aa1` (head `c38391877d7ad06db385936e0838df4c26e78171`, ledger commit `c93128f7e`); PR #2150 is merged as `e76a38a4468cdc8d402842cc9657c8681e35d912`.

- **Change:** the runtime now builds the constraint, calendar-assignment, resource-assignment, resource-period and schedule-settings PostgreSQL repositories, one `AuthoritativeScheduleInputComposer` → `AuthoritativeScheduleSnapshotPublisher` → `P6ScheduleSnapshotPublicationAPI` chain, and the canonical `AuthoritativeScheduleQueryProvider` → `PublishedScheduleFactsProvider` → workspace read. One `build_postgres_calendar_registry` factory serves both the query and publication. The workbench data-date provider reuses the same settings repository. **No migration, no second calculator, no browser-side CPM, no policy/RLS/authentication change.**
- **Robustness fix in the registry:** a calendar master with no stored definition is skipped instead of aborting the registry. Before this, one empty calendar created through `POST /p6/calendars` would have broken the schedule read of every project in the database. Publication still fails closed for a *referenced* calendar with no definition because the composer rejects it.
- **Evidence (live PostgreSQL, production WSGI composition):** durable WBS + two activities + FS relationship + stored calendar definition + saved settings → publish 200/201 with `snapshot_hash` and `calculation_identity` → workspace read returns `activity.early_start/early_finish/late_start/late_finish`, `activity.total_float` = 0, `activity.free_float`, `activity.is_critical` = true, gantt `critical`, and successor start ≥ predecessor finish → a new login reads identical numbers → snapshot reads are revision-bound (other revision raises `REVISION_CONFLICT`, other tenant returns `None`) → stale workspace revision 409 `STALE_WORKSPACE_READ_SCOPE` → viewer read 200 / publish 403 / foreign tenant 403 → publication fails closed on an undefined calendar while the published snapshot still projects → the pre-change provider shape exposes no float or date.
- **Regression guards proven by reverting:** removing `schedule_facts_provider` fails the test on `activity.early_start`; removing the publication API fails with `404 in {200, 201}`.
- **Tests:** full Python suite with isolated PostgreSQL 14 — **5,562 passed / 20 skipped / 15 subtests**; operationalization live module **17 passed**. No web change, so web tests/typecheck/build are unchanged.
- **Not changed:** no production/Supabase database was touched, no migration was applied outside the isolated local engine, and CUBI-REF-001/002/003/CUBI-DAM-001 were not read, written or deleted.
- **Honest limits:** the immutable definition of a root calendar has no HTTP endpoint yet (the router creates masters only), so the test writes that definition through the same durable repository the application uses; that endpoint gap is recorded as the next calendar work. Multiple float-path membership stays absent (asserted absent, never fabricated). Rescheduling, resources/costs, reports and closeout remain unwired.
- **Status:** PR #2152 OPEN; merge only after every CI job is green.
