## 2026-11-24 — P0 Engineering Phase E (fifth slice): a root calendar is defined through the authenticated API — Issue #2138

- **main SHA at slice start:** `49610a27eef95549e89b640526915c329aa1eed8` (verified against `origin/main` before editing; the slice-4 merge). **Correction carried forward:** slice 4 recorded PR #2152 as OPEN; it is **MERGED** as `49610a27eef95549e89b640526915c329aa1eed8` (head `58972f809e1d224e8808c163a28b228c5852bf6b`).
- **Branch:** `feat/engineering-calendar-definition`.
- **Defect:** production could create a calendar master through the router but had no authenticated way to define its working week, date system or holidays. The slice-4 live test therefore seeded the definition through `PostgresCalendarSnapshotRepository`, and the only user-facing route to a usable calendar was "inherit from a base calendar". A master created in the product UI with per-weekday work-hour rules still had no stored definition, so the evaluator registry skipped it and no schedule could use it.
- **Contract added (`p6-calendar-definition.v1`, additive):**
  - `POST /api/projects/:id/p6/calendars` accepts an optional `definition` object (`system`, `working_weekdays`, `holidays`, optional `daily_intervals`/`time_period_factors`). Master and immutable definition commit in one application transaction; a rejected definition leaves no master behind.
  - `GET /api/projects/:id/p6/calendars/:id/:version/definition` returns the exact canonical definition the evaluator consumes plus `schedulable`. A bare master answers `definition: null`, `schedulable: false` — never an invented working week.
  - `PUT .../definition` seals a master with its first definition under `expected_revision` (bumps the master revision) and is idempotent for the identical definition. A different definition is rejected with 409 `CALENDAR_DEFINITION_IMMUTABLE`, because a published snapshot references that exact version; a real change must create a new calendar version.
- **Error mapping:** `REVISION_CONFLICT`, `CALENDAR_DEFINITION_IMMUTABLE` and `CALENDAR_ALREADY_EXISTS` answer **409**; `CALENDAR_NOT_FOUND` answers **404**; every other persistence failure keeps the existing opaque `P6_CALENDAR_REQUEST_INVALID` code (no internal text leaks), while definition validation codes such as `CALENDAR_DEFINITION_WORKING_WEEK_REQUIRED` surface so the caller can correct the payload.
- **Fail-closed rules:** unknown definition fields are rejected (a typo cannot silently change the calendar); an empty working week is rejected; a `working-time` calendar whose weekdays exceed Monday–Friday must supply explicit intervals instead of inheriting an implicit 08:00–17:00 week; a definition and a base calendar are mutually exclusive; the date system cannot silently switch between master and definition.
- **Web (real product path):** the Calendar Register dialog can now create a **root** calendar — the base select has a "Root calendar (no base)" option and the dialog collects the working week. The client refuses a root create without a working week (`CALENDAR_WORKING_WEEK_REQUIRED`) and sends `definition` together with the master, so no repository write is needed outside the router. `New` is no longer disabled when the catalog is empty, since the first calendar can now be a root.
- **Evidence (live PostgreSQL 14, production WSGI composition, no repository seed):** authenticated create with definition → 200 with `schedulable: true` and revision 1 → fresh-session `GET .../definition` returns the identical canonical definition → durable `calendar_master` row and `calendar_master_snapshot` jsonb row (`working_weekdays [1,2,3,4,5]`, holiday `2026-11-04`) → identical PUT is idempotent → changed PUT is 409 `CALENDAR_DEFINITION_IMMUTABLE` → bare master is `schedulable: false` with `definition: null` → stale `PUT expected_revision 2` is 409 `REVISION_CONFLICT`, correct revision seals to revision 2, replay at revision 1 is 409 → malformed definition is 400 `CALENDAR_DEFINITION_WORKING_WEEK_REQUIRED` **with zero master rows** → viewer reads and is denied on write (403), foreign tenant 403 → `build_postgres_calendar_registry` resolves the stored definition and answers `is_working_day` correctly for Monday (false), Tuesday (true), the holiday (false) and Saturday (true) → settings bound to that calendar → publication 200/201 with hash and identity → the same snapshot queried again from a **new session** returns identical facts → the workspace read reports `activity.early_start = 2026-11-03`, proving the Monday project start was moved by the API-defined working week, with float present.
- **Unit evidence:** a storage failure after the master insert rolls the master back (no half-created calendar); definition parser rejects empty weeks, unknown fields and missing time intervals; sealing is revision-guarded and immutable.
- **Tests:** full Python suite with isolated PostgreSQL 14 — **5,568 passed / 20 skipped / 15 subtests**; operationalization live module **18 passed**; calendar/route unit tests **170 passed**. Web: `npm test` **1,352 + 16 passed**, `npm run typecheck` and `npm run build` exit 0.
- **Not changed:** no migration, no second calendar model, no auth/RLS change. No production/Supabase write, no Showcase record (CUBI-REF-001/002/003, CUBI-DAM-001) read, written or deleted.
- **Honest limits:** a calendar's definition cannot be edited in place (by design: definitions are immutable per version, and creating a new version from a stored definition is the sanctioned path — the copy dialog already exists, but it copies to a *new* calendar reference rather than adding a version to the same calendar id); working-time calendars are accepted by the API but the web dialog creates working-day root calendars only; resource calendars and assignment editing still use the base-inheritance flow.
- **Status:** PR #2153 MERGED as `543e79860d655f891a897dc687e0ae3577b3b936`. Next dependency in the same mission: resources and activity resource assignments must be reachable and persisted through the production runtime, and their demand must actually drive the published schedule.

## 2026-11-24 — P0 Engineering Phase E (sixth slice): Resources and Activity Resource Assignments on the production runtime — Issue #2138

- **main SHA at branch start:** `543e79860d655f891a897dc687e0ae3577b3b936` (verified against `origin/main`; the #2153 merge).
- **Branch:** `feat/engineering-resources-production-runtime`.
- **Changed files:** `src/construction_pm/deployment/production_runtime.py`, `src/construction_pm/http/production_composition.py`, `src/construction_pm/deployment/migrations.py`, `src/construction_pm/http/project_lifecycle_routes.py`, `tests/integration/test_cubi_database_operationalization_postgres_live.py`, `tests/test_authoritative_schedule_snapshot_composer.py`, `tests/test_resource_production_composition.py` (new).
- **Audit result:** every PostgreSQL resource repository and every HTTP/API adapter already existed (`PostgresP6ResourceRepository`, assignment, assignment-period, spread, capacity and rate repositories; `P6ResourceAPI`, `P6ResourceReadAPI`, `P6ResourceWriteAPI`, spread/capacity/rate read+write APIs; all `/p6/resources`, `/p6/resource-assignments`, `/p6/resource-assignment-periods`, `/p6/resource-spreads`, `/p6/resource-capacities`, `/p6/resource-rates` router branches). The gap was purely composition: `ProductionRuntime._build()` passed none of them to `build_project_lifecycle_wsgi_app`, so in production every one of those routes answered `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` and the composer could still read assignment rows written only by tests.
- **Composition fix (no second model, no second store):** the runtime now constructs the resource master/capacity/rate/spread repositories, the assignment and period application services **over the same `PostgresP6ResourceAssignmentRepository` / `PostgresP6ResourceAssignmentPeriodRepository` instances the schedule composer already reads**, and passes `p6_resource_api`, `resource_read_api`, `resource_write_api`, `p6_resource_spread_api`, capacity/rate read+write APIs and the four resource-calendar repositories to the lifecycle seam. `production_composition.py` accepts and forwards `p6_resource_spread_api`; passing `resource_read_api` without the calendar repositories is rejected with `RESOURCE_LEVELING_RESOLVER_DEPENDENCIES_INCOMPLETE`, so a half-wired resolver cannot boot.
- **Scheduling fix (proof that persisted assignments are consumed):** the runtime's own `AuthoritativeScheduleQueryProvider` (the provider behind Workspace Read via `PublishedScheduleFactsProvider`) had no `leveling_input_resolver`. A snapshot carrying resource-dependent activity facts therefore failed closed with `SCHEDULE_RESOURCE_INPUT_REQUIRED` on `/api/v2/workspace/control-room/read`. It is now built with `build_persisted_resource_leveling_input_resolver` over `resource_read_api` and the durable calendar repositories — the same resolver the Schedule Query composition uses, reading the same assignment and period tables the HTTP writes target.
- **Migration:** **18** — `resource-spread-buckets` (revision 1, additive). `P6ResourceReadAPI` structurally requires a spread service and the router exposes `/p6/resource-spreads`, but `p6_resource_spread_bucket` was absent from the 17-step production registry. The step calls the existing `PostgresP6ResourceSpreadRepository.initialize()` (no new schema model) and extends the existing Revoke + restrictive-RLS hardening to the new table. Verified on a probe schema: ledger 18, applied 18, 99 tables, drift `None`; applied to no production database.
- **Access-control correction:** the capacity and rate **read** routes let `ProjectLifecycleError(PROJECT_NOT_FOUND_OR_NOT_AUTHORIZED)` fall through to their `ValueError` branch, so a foreign-tenant caller received **400** while every neighbouring resource route answers **403**. All four read branches now map that error to 403 exactly like the write branches.
- **Live evidence (production WSGI + isolated PostgreSQL 14, no repository seeding):** authenticated project and two API-created calendars with `working_weekdays [0,1,2,3,4]` → resource POST without a calendar is 409 `RESOURCE_CALENDAR_NOT_FOUND`, with a calendar returns revision 1 plus a `p6_resource` row → WBS child and a Resource Dependent activity (planned duration 2) created through HTTP → Resource Dependent settings saved through the API using the read `record_revision` → assignment POST (units 40, units/time 8/day, driving) returns one assignment id; replaying the same id with a different payload is 409 `IMMUTABLE_RESOURCE_ASSIGNMENT`; an incomplete units/time pair is 400 → publishing with no period rows is 400 `RESOURCE_DEPENDENT_ASSIGNMENT_PERIODS_REQUIRED` → five daily period rows (8 units each) → publish 201 with hash and calculation identity → **Workspace Read reports `duration` 5 and `activity.early_finish` `2026-11-06` while `activity.planned_duration` stays `"2"`**, and `/api/v1/schedule/query` returns the same computed facts → fresh login reload returns the identical assignment id, five period rows, the same query facts and the same workspace cells → capacity write resolves `8` on a day basis, rate write resolves `1200` per day in USD → resource PATCH at revision 1 is revision 2, stale revision 1 is 409 `REVISION_CONFLICT`, deactivate is revision 3 (`is_active false`), restore is revision 4 with name/calendar/default units intact → viewer GET 200 and viewer writes 403 → foreign tenant 403 on resources, resource, assignments, assignment, capacities and rates → capacity write with `expected_revision = project_revision + 1` is 409 `REVISION_CONFLICT`.
- **Unit evidence:** `tests/test_resource_production_composition.py` proves an unwired composition answers `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND` for resources and assignments and a wired one persists a resource (revision 1) and an assignment readable back identically; `tests/test_authoritative_schedule_snapshot_composer.py` gains `test_resource_assignment_for_unknown_activity_fails_publication` (an assignment pointing at a missing activity blocks publication with `RESOURCE_ASSIGNMENT_UNKNOWN_ACTIVITY`).
- **Tests:** full Python suite with isolated PostgreSQL 14 — **5,572 passed / 20 skipped / 15 subtests**; operationalization live module **19 passed**; resource/composer/route/query suites **231 passed**. Web unchanged: `npm test` **1,352 + 16 passed**, `npm run typecheck` exit 0.
- **Not changed:** no auth/RLS change, no second calculator or client-side CPM, no production/Supabase write, no Showcase record (CUBI-REF-001/002/003, CUBI-DAM-001) read, written or deleted.
- **Honest limits:** assignment "update" keeps the immutable semantics already enforced by the API (a change is a new assignment id; `-R2` in the web client) and "delete" is deactivate/restore on the master, not a hard delete of demand history; resource spread buckets are now stored and served but no cost or report consumer reads them yet; a resource-dependent snapshot still fails closed when its period rows are missing (by design) and the leveling-requested branch was not exercised in this slice; the live proof runs on an isolated schema, not against the owner production database.
- **Status:** PR open; merge only after every CI job is green. Next dependency in the same mission (after independent acceptance): Data Date & Progress, then Rescheduling, Costs, Reports and Closeout in real dependency order.

## 2026-11-25 — P0 Engineering Phase E (seventh slice): Data Date and Progress on the production runtime — Issue #2138

- **main SHA at branch start:** `3e789f64bfdb87082c264a36bd4e9fb796a1006d` (verified against `origin/main` before editing; the #2154 merge). **Correction carried forward:** slice 6 recorded PR #2154 as open; it is **MERGED** as `3e789f64bfdb87082c264a36bd4e9fb796a1006d` (head `b7e6f21db`).
- **Branch / head:** `feat/engineering-data-date-progress`, feature commit `67213b92d4fee9edbcef3534af228099ffa931dc`. PR **#2155**.
- **Audit result:** Data Date already had a durable path (`ScheduleOptions.data_date` → `PostgresProjectScheduleSettingsRepository` → `POST /p6/schedule-settings`, and the workbench data-date provider) and the shared scheduler already honoured it (`forward_pass` bounds remaining work at `max(logic_start, actual_start, data_date)` and `schedule_evaluator` passes it into `time_schedule`). Progress writes already had `P6ActivityProgressAPI` with an append-only event store and out-of-sequence review. The production gap was composition plus one missing table: `ProductionRuntime._build()` passed neither `activity_progress_history_repository` nor `activity_progress_transaction_manager`, so `P6ActivityProgressAPI` was `None`; the history route answered `P6_ACTIVITY_PROGRESS_HISTORY_UNAVAILABLE`, PATCH silently skipped event recording, and `activity_progress_event` was absent from the migration registry.
- **Change (no new model, no second engine):** migration **19** `activity-progress-history` calls the existing `PostgresActivityProgressHistoryRepository.initialize()` (no new schema model) and extends REVOKE + restrictive-RLS hardening to the table. `ProductionRuntime` now builds that repository and passes it, with the request `_RequestTransactionManager`, into `build_project_lifecycle_wsgi_app`, so the immutable event is appended in the same transaction as the Activity Master save and the out-of-sequence review is evaluated against the stored relationships and activities.
- **Composition evidence (`tests/test_progress_production_composition.py`, 6 tests):** unwired app → history route 400 `P6_ACTIVITY_PROGRESS_HISTORY_UNAVAILABLE` and PATCH leaves the store empty; partial wiring (repository without transaction manager) → `ACTIVITY_PROGRESS_HISTORY_DEPENDENCIES_INCOMPLETE`; wired PATCH → revision 2, event `A-1@2` with `actual_start/percent_complete/remaining_duration/status/status_code` changes, `percent_complete_trend`, and `FS_START_BEFORE_PREDECESSOR_FINISH`; stale revision → 409 `REVISION_CONFLICT` with the history still one event; viewer reads but 403 on write; foreign tenant 404 on history and activity with no write.
- **Live PostgreSQL acceptance (production WSGI, isolated schema, no repository seeding):** independent project through `POST /api/projects` → project and resource calendars defined through `POST /p6/calendars` (`schedulable: true`) → WBS + A→B→C FS chain → resource and assignment on C coexist with progress → Data Date `2026-11-16` stored and read back → Completed A (Actual Start 2026-11-02, Actual Finish 2026-11-04, 100%, Remaining 0) and In Progress B (Actual Start 2026-11-05, 40%, Remaining 2) → `/progress-history` returns event revision 1→2 with the 40.0 trend → the event is read directly from `activity_progress_event` through `PostgresActivityProgressHistoryRepository` → stale PATCH 409 with one event → publish 200/201 → Workspace Read: B keeps Actual Start `2026-11-05`, forecast finish ≥ `2026-11-16`, gantt `progressPercent 40`; clearing the Data Date finishes **earlier**, restoring it reproduces the **identical** finish (proving the stored Data Date drives the shared core); B and C total float 0 with `is_critical` true; `activity.planned_duration` still `"3"/"2"/"2"` and no `activity.baseline*` cells → a new login reads the same Data Date, Actual Finish, 40%, Remaining 2, Planned Durations, early start and float → viewer history 200 / PATCH 403; foreign tenant history and PATCH 403 → stale schedule-settings save 409 `REVISION_CONFLICT`.
- **Tests:** full Python suite with DSN unset for the bulk run — **5,024 passed / 85 skipped / 15 subtests**; operationalization live module with isolated PostgreSQL 14 — **20 passed**; all `*postgres_live` integration modules — **153 passed**; progress/activity/settings/resource live repositories — **12 passed**. Web unchanged: `npm test` **1,352 + 16 passed**, `npm run typecheck` and `npm run build` exit 0.
- **Not changed:** no auth/RLS weakening, no second scheduling/calendar/persistence engine, no production or Supabase write, and no Showcase record (CUBI-REF-001/002/003, CUBI-DAM-001) read, written or deleted. Migration 19 was applied only to throw-away local schemas.
- **Honest limits:** the live proof runs on an isolated schema, not the owner production database; progress edits still use the Activity Master PATCH contract until a dedicated progress dialog route is accepted; resource-unit progress (Resource Dependent) keeps its stricter composer rules and was not re-driven in this slice.
- **Status:** PR #2155 open; merge only after every CI job is green. Next dependency in the same mission: Rescheduling and Schedule Variance, then Costs & Budget Integration, Reports/Dashboard Integration and Project Closeout/Reopening.

## 2026-11-25 — P0 Engineering Phase E (eighth slice): Rescheduling and Schedule Variance on the production runtime — Issue #2138

- **main SHA at branch start:** `5d10a780` (verified against `origin/main`; the #2156 docs merge that followed #2155). **Correction carried forward:** slice 7 recorded PR #2155 as open; it is **MERGED** as `6ce2838a27d796da2732d82e76a931fe4f6b18b5`.
- **Branch / head:** `feat/engineering-rescheduling-schedule-variance`, feature commit `59190519`. PR to be opened from this branch.
- **Audit result:** the durable lifecycle already existed and was sound — `SchedulingJobService` (submit/claim/renew/checkpoint/complete/fail/cancel/recover_expired with revision CAS), `PostgresSchedulingJobRepository` (single-flight partial index `ux_scheduling_jobs_active_single_flight` on `state IN ('QUEUED','LEASED')`, immutable results), `SchedulingJobAPI` (submit/status/result/cancel/worker status/tick), `SchedulingEngineJobRunner` and `SchedulingJobExecutor` over the one shared scheduling engine, plus the baseline capture/read stack (`P6BaselineCaptureAPI`, `P6BaselineAPI`, `P6BaselineActivityAPI`, `p6_baseline`, `p6_baseline_activity`). The production gaps were: no migration created `scheduling_jobs`/`scheduling_job_results`, and `ProductionRuntime._build()` passed neither the job repository nor the baseline APIs, so every rescheduling route was inert and no baseline could be captured or read.
- **Change (no second engine, no second model):** migration **20** `scheduling-jobs` calls the existing `PostgresSchedulingJobRepository.initialize()` and extends the existing REVOKE + restrictive-RLS hardening to `scheduling_jobs` and `scheduling_job_results`. `ProductionRuntime` now builds `PostgresSchedulingJobRepository`, `PostgresP6BaselineRepository`, `PostgresP6BaselineActivityRepository`, `PostgresP6ExpenseRepository`, the baseline capture service over `PublishedScheduleCaptureSource(PublishedScheduleFactsProvider(...))`, and the baseline read APIs, and passes them through `build_project_lifecycle_wsgi_app` together with `scheduling_execution_mode="inline"` and `scheduling_worker_token` from `CONSTRUCTION_PM_SCHEDULING_WORKER_TOKEN` (absent token ⇒ worker routes 404, never anonymous execution).
- **Execution-mode hazard found and fixed:** every repository resolves the request-scoped `RequestConnection` ContextVar. A worker thread does not inherit that context, so thread-mode execution could claim a job while its repository looked at a different connection — the live tick test hung with PostgreSQL `idle in transaction`. A new **`inline`** mode executes `_run_job` on the request thread, creates no heartbeat thread (the lease is only meaningful across processes/threads) and is selected by the production runtime. It is not a second scheduler: claim, lease, revision CAS, single-flight index, immutable results and the worker token all still apply, and `thread`/`process` remain available for deployments that build per-worker providers. Runner/executor reject process-only settings under inline and still require a provider for thread/inline.
- **Access-control correction:** rescheduling `POST` (submit and cancel) now authorizes `Permission.PROJECT_WRITE`; `GET` status/result stay readable for viewers through `open_project`. Cross-tenant open/authorize failure still maps to 403.
- **Live evidence (production WSGI + isolated PostgreSQL 14, no repository seeding):** authenticated independent project → working-week calendar, WBS and A→B FS chain (Planned Durations 4/3, stored Planned Finishes 2026-11-10 / 2026-11-14) → Data Date `2026-11-16` saved through `/p6/schedule/settings` → publish snapshot → submit job returns **202 QUEUED** with `job_id`, snapshot and project revision in `run_parameters_json` → duplicate submit is **200 deduplicated** with the same `job_id` → the row is read directly from `scheduling_jobs` as `QUEUED` → result before execution is **409 `SCHEDULING_JOB_NOT_SUCCEEDED`** → a real worker tick with `X-Worker-Token` claims and succeeds **1** → status `SUCCEEDED`, revision ≥ 2, result `output_hash` 64 hex, `activity_count` 2 → `scheduling_jobs` row is exactly `("SUCCEEDED", 1, revision)` and `scheduling_job_results` stores the same engine version, calculation identity and hash → a second tick claims **0** and succeeds **0** (no recalculation) → a new login retrieves the identical hash → a new submission after success is a **new generation** (`job_id` differs) and cancels to `CANCELLED` → baseline `BL-RSCH` captured from published facts (201, `counts.activities` 2) with stored windows A `2026-11-02–2026-11-05`, B `2026-11-06–2026-11-10` → PATCH A to Actual Start `2026-11-10`, 60 %, Remaining 2, In Progress → republish → Workspace Read keeps `activity.planned_duration` `"4"/"3"`; master keeps `duration_value` 4 and `planned_finish` 2026-11-10, and reports 60 % / In Progress / Actual Start 2026-11-10 → Tracking reports `planned_finish` unchanged, `finish` ≥ 2026-11-16 and `finish_variance_days == (finish − planned_finish).days > 0` → clearing the Data Date finishes **earlier** with a smaller variance, restoring it reproduces the **identical** finish and variance (the stored Data Date drives the variance) → the raw `p6_baseline_activity` rows are byte-for-byte unchanged before and after progress → viewer GET status/result **200** but submit/cancel **403 `AUTHORIZATION_PERMISSION_DENIED`** → another tenant gets **403** on the job and on the baseline list.
- **Concurrency/single-flight evidence:** duplicate submit dedup, the partial unique index, lease/CAS lifecycle tests (`tests/scheduling/test_scheduling_job_lifecycle.py`, `tests/scheduling/test_scheduling_job_executor.py`, `tests/test_scheduling_job_postgres.py`) and the second-tick-claims-nothing assertion together prove an incompatible concurrent execution cannot run the same job twice.
- **Tests:** full Python suite with DSN unset for the bulk run — **5,279 passed / 264 skipped / 15 subtests**; operationalization live module with isolated PostgreSQL 14 — **21 passed** (including the new rescheduling/variance test); scheduling suites (`tests/scheduling` + job HTTP + job Postgres) — **1,686 passed**; production readiness/web-app/lifecycle-wsgi/routes/migration-parity/tracking-composition — **200 passed**. Migration probe: ledger 20, applied 20, 102 tables, drift `None`. Web unchanged.
- **Not changed:** no auth/RLS weakening, no second scheduling engine or second baseline model, no production or Supabase write, and no Showcase record (CUBI-REF-001/002/003, CUBI-DAM-001) read, written or deleted. Migration 20 was applied only to throw-away local schemas.
- **Honest limits:** the live proof runs on an isolated schema, not the owner production database; the worker is driven by an authenticated tick, so a deployment still needs an external scheduler to call it (the token route exists, no daemon was added); inline execution binds a job to one request, so a very long calculation occupies that request rather than a background pool; `scheduling_jobs`/`scheduling_job_results` now exist, but no retention/purge policy is defined; multi-baseline comparison remains out of scope.
- **Status:** PR open; merge only after every CI job is green. Next dependency in the same mission: Costs and Budget Integration, then Reports/Dashboard Integration and Project Closeout/Reopening.

## 2026-11-25 — P0 Engineering Phase E (ninth slice): Costs — the expense register on the production runtime — Issue #2138

- **main SHA at branch start:** `c1236d05a1e9827b17c07cadcbb723db38cae527` (verified against `origin/main`; the #2157 merge). **Correction carried forward:** slice 8 recorded PR #2157 as open; it is **MERGED** as `c1236d05a1e9827b17c07cadcbb723db38cae527`.
- **Branch / head:** `feat/engineering-costs-expense-register`, feature commit `4e5d9856`. PR to be opened from this branch.
- **Audit result:** the cost chain already existed — `P6Expense` / `PostgresP6ExpenseRepository` (revision-scoped, insert-once and immutable per expense id), `P6ExpenseAPI` (PROJECT_READ / PROJECT_WRITE), the `/p6/expenses` router branch, and `P6BaselineCaptureService`, which rolls activity-linked `planned_cost` into the baseline window and reports WBS-only expense as unattributed. `p6_expense` was already created by the reference-engineering step (14) and covered by the direct-API hardening step (16), so **no migration was needed** — a candidate step 21 was written, found redundant against the structural fingerprint (102 tables unchanged), and removed instead of adding ledger churn. The real gaps were: `ProductionRuntime._build()` never passed `p6_expense_api`, so every `/p6/expenses` request answered `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`; the list branch read `activity_id`/`wbs_id` from a request **body** a GET never carries, and the WSGI app dropped the query string for that path; POST authorized only `open_project`, so write permission was enforced late; and an immutable/revision conflict surfaced as a generic 400.
- **Change (no second cost model):** the runtime builds `P6ExpenseAPI(P6ExpenseApplicationService(expense_repository, transaction_manager), policy)` over the **same** `PostgresP6ExpenseRepository` that baseline capture reads and passes it through the lifecycle seam. The WSGI app now carries the query string for `/p6/expenses`; the route parses `activity_id`/`wbs_id` from it, accepts a bodyless GET, requires `Permission.PROJECT_WRITE` for POST at the route boundary, maps `IMMUTABLE_EXPENSE` and `REVISION_CONFLICT` to **409**, other persistence failures to 400 and `AuthorizationError` to 403.
- **Live evidence (production WSGI + isolated PostgreSQL 14, no repository seeding):** independent project → working-week calendar, WBS and one 3-day activity → schedule settings and a published snapshot → empty register reads `[]` without a body → activity-linked expense `1500.25` planned / `0` actual / `1500.25` remaining USD and a WBS-only expense `100` USD are accepted at the project revision → `?activity_id=` returns only the activity expense, `?wbs_id=` returns only the WBS expense → a fresh session reads the identical `remaining_cost` → identical replay is **200**, a changed payload under the same id is **409 `IMMUTABLE_EXPENSE`** → the `p6_expense` rows are exactly the two stored facts at the current revision → baseline capture reports `counts.budgeted_cost = 1500.25`, one activity row with `budgeted_cost = 1500.25` / `currency = USD`, and `unattributed_expense_ids = [WBS expense]` instead of silently dropping it → the activity keeps `duration_value = 3` and `planned_finish = 2026-11-04`, proving cost does not move the schedule → viewer GET list/detail **200** but POST **403 `AUTHORIZATION_PERMISSION_DENIED`** → another tenant gets **403** on read and write → the final table holds exactly the two rows, so the denied writes created nothing.
- **Contract correction:** an immutable replay now answers **409** (`IMMUTABLE_EXPENSE`) instead of 400, matching resource assignments and baselines; `tests/test_preview_expenses.py` was updated to the same conflict code because it exercises the shared router.
- **Unit evidence:** `tests/test_p6_expense_http_routes.py` now proves query-string filters reach the API, a bodyless GET works, a viewer reads but cannot create, and `IMMUTABLE_EXPENSE` / `REVISION_CONFLICT` answer 409.
- **Tests:** full Python suite with DSN unset for the bulk run — **5,283 passed / 265 skipped / 15 subtests**; operationalization live module with isolated PostgreSQL 14 — **22 passed**; expense/baseline/wsgi/lifecycle-route suites with DSN — **147 passed**; preview expense module — **3 passed**. Web unchanged.
- **Not changed:** no migration, no auth/RLS weakening, no second cost model, no production or Supabase write, and no Showcase record (CUBI-REF-001/002/003, CUBI-DAM-001) read, written or deleted.
- **Honest limits:** an expense is immutable per id, so a correction is a new expense id — there is no update or delete endpoint; an expense that points at a non-existent activity is accepted by the register and is not diagnosed by baseline capture (it simply finds no row), which the next cost slice should report; expenses are revision-scoped like every P6 fact, so a workspace revision change makes earlier cost unreadable until it is carried forward; the budget register (`project_budget_*`) still has no HTTP surface in production (only the importer writes it via `PostgresBudgetStore`), and the legacy `/cost/*` `CostAPI` remains preview-only — both belong to Budget Integration.
- **Status:** PR open; merge only after every CI job is green. Next dependency in the same mission: Budget Integration, then Reports/Dashboard Integration and Project Closeout/Reopening.

## 2026-11-25 — P0 Engineering Phase E (tenth slice): Budget Integration — the register on the production runtime — Issue #2138

- **main SHA at branch start:** `beedbeefffbabdf41cdf30414d3028ea1fac19b5` (verified against `origin/main`; the #2158 merge). **Correction carried forward:** slice 9 recorded PR #2158 as open; it is **MERGED** as `beedbeefffbabdf41cdf30414d3028ea1fac19b5`.
- **Branch / head:** `feat/engineering-budget-integration`, feature commit `e078e27f`. PR to be opened from this branch.
- **Audit result:** the budget surface already existed end to end — `BudgetLine` / `BudgetChange` / `FundingSource`, `BudgetApplicationService` (PROJECT_READ/PROJECT_WRITE, admin-only approval, derived summary), `BudgetAPI`, and full `/budget/lines`, `/budget/changes`, `/budget/funding`, `/budget/summary`, `/budget/lines/{id}/history` routing gated on `budget_api`. The four `project_budget_*` tables are created by the reference-enterprise step (15) and hardened by the direct-API lock step (16), so **no migration was needed**. Two real gaps: `ProductionRuntime` never passed `budget_api`, so every budget route answered `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`; and `PostgresBudgetStore` overrode only `persist_line` / `get_line` / `list_lines` — `update_line`, `list_changes`, `create_change`, `get_change`, `transition_change`, `list_funding`, `persist_funding`, `update_funding`, `line_history`, `_get_funding` and `_audit` were inherited from `SQLiteBudgetStore` and used `?` placeholders and TEXT timestamps, which cannot run on PostgreSQL.
- **Change (no second budget model):** the missing operations are implemented on `PostgresBudgetStore` with `%s` placeholders, JSON operators, `TIMESTAMPTZ` audit rows and PostgreSQL duplicate-key detection, preserving the preview store's revision CAS, idempotency fingerprints, insert-once line/change/funding ids and immutable audit trail. The runtime builds `BudgetAPI(BudgetApplicationService(PostgresBudgetStore(connection), policy, transaction_manager))` and passes it through the lifecycle seam. The same store is handed to `ProjectMetricReader`, so a `budget_balance` threshold observes the rows the API writes instead of failing closed; `expense_actual_total` stays disconnected because that reader path expects the legacy preview cost store, not the P6 expense register.
- **Live evidence (production WSGI + isolated PostgreSQL 14, no repository seeding):** independent project → `POST /budget/lines` with `Idempotency-Key` stores `1000.00` USD at revision 1 and an identical replay returns revision 1 → a different payload under the same key is **409 `BUDGET_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSE`** → a new session reads the line → `?currency=USD&search=civil` returns it and `?currency=EUR` returns none → `PATCH` to `1200.00` at revision 1 returns revision 2 and a stale revision 1 is **409 `BUDGET_REVISION_CONFLICT`** → two pending changes (`250.00`, `100.00`) are visible but excluded from `current_budget` → a planner approval is **403 `BUDGET_APPROVAL_REQUIRES_ADMIN`**, an admin approval returns revision 2 → funding `500.00` then revision 2 `700.00` → summary reads `original 1200.00 / approved 250.00 / current 1450.00 / pending 100.00 / funding 700.00 / balance 750.00` → line history is exactly `created` (1) then `updated` (2) with timestamps → changes read back `CH-1 approved 2`, `CH-2 pending 1` → `project_budget_line` holds exactly `(BL-1, 2)` → a `budget_balance` threshold with upper `1000.00` monitors to `observed 750` / `normal`, proving the threshold reads the same rows → viewer reads lines/summary **200** but create is **403 `BUDGET_PERMISSION_DENIED`** → another tenant is **403** and the table still holds exactly one line.
- **Unit/live store evidence:** `tests/integration/test_budget_postgres_live.py` proves line/change/funding round trips, idempotent replays, key reuse, JSON-filtered listing, revision CAS conflicts, pending-to-approved transition rules, ordered audit timestamps and transactional rollback on real PostgreSQL.
- **Tests:** full Python suite with DSN unset for the bulk run — **5,283 passed / 268 skipped / 15 subtests**; operationalization live module with isolated PostgreSQL 14 — **23 passed**; budget + threshold modules (API/application/HTTP/SQLite/Postgres/preview) — **49 passed**; budget PostgreSQL live module — **2 passed**. Web unchanged.
- **Not changed:** no migration, no auth/RLS weakening, no second budget model, no production or Supabase write, and no Showcase record (CUBI-REF-001/002/003, CUBI-DAM-001) read, written or deleted. The legacy `/cost/*` `CostAPI` (SQLite) remains preview-only and is not duplicated.
- **Honest limits:** `expense_actual_total` thresholds remain disconnected (the reader expects the legacy preview cost store, while the deployed expense register is the P6 model) — that adapter is the next cost step if the parameter is wanted; budget lines reference `cost_account_id` as a string with no existence check; budget revisions are their own sequence, independent of the project workspace revision; there is no currency conversion or cross-currency summary beyond one row per currency; the budget register is not yet consumed by the earned-value read model or by reports.
- **Status:** PR open; merge only after every CI job is green. Next dependency in the same mission: Reports/Dashboard Integration, then Project Closeout/Reopening and the Integrated Engineering Acceptance.

## 2026-11-25 — P0 Engineering Phase E (eleventh slice): Reports and Earned Value on the production runtime — Issue #2138

- **main SHA at branch start:** `5bf53943e311b9951819aa0b60dd4d3ba9231b8a` (verified against `origin/main`; the #2159 merge). **Correction carried forward:** slice 10 recorded PR #2159 as open; it is **MERGED** as `5bf53943e311b9951819aa0b60dd4d3ba9231b8a`.
- **Branch / head:** `feat/engineering-reports-earned-value`, feature commit `af52c284`. PR to be opened from this branch.
- **Audit result:** the report domain already existed end to end — `ReportDefinition` / `ReportRun` / `ReportAuditEvent` / `PostgresReportStore` (definitions, immutable runs, idempotency, audit), `ReportApplicationService` (PROJECT_READ/PROJECT_WRITE, bound parameters, dataset over stored facts), `ReportAPI` (catalogue, definitions CRUD, run, runs, export, schedules), `ReportScheduleService` / `PostgresReportScheduleStore` (cadence, due claim, revision CAS, audit) and the full `/reports` router branch gated on `report_api`; the earned-value read model (`P6EarnedValueReadModelService`/`P6EarnedValueReadModelAPI` and the `/p6/earned-value` branch) and its dependencies were also present. The production gaps were composition and one missing table set: `ProductionRuntime._build()` passed neither `report_api` nor `p6_earned_value_read_api`, so both surfaces answered `ROUTE_NOT_FOUND`, and no migration created the five `project_report_*` tables, so a wired surface would have failed on missing relations.
- **Change (no second report engine, no second measurement):** migration **21** `report-register-and-schedules` calls the existing `PostgresReportStore.initialize()` and `PostgresReportScheduleStore.initialize()` (no new schema model) and extends the existing REVOKE + restrictive-RLS hardening to `project_report_definition`, `project_report_audit`, `project_report_idempotency`, `project_report_run` and `project_report_schedule`. A candidate step for `p6_activity_period_actual` was written, found already created by the reference-engineering step (14) and hardened by step 16 (probe stayed at the same table set), and removed instead of adding ledger churn. `ProductionRuntime` now builds `ReportAPI(ReportApplicationService(PostgresReportStore, AuthoritativeReportDataset(activity_repository, resource_repository, risk_store), policy, transaction_manager), schedules=ReportScheduleService(PostgresReportScheduleStore, report_store, policy, transaction_manager))` and `P6EarnedValueReadModelAPI(P6EarnedValueReadModelService(...), policy)` over the **same** baseline-activity, activity, expense, actuals and WBS repositories plus a scope-aware resolver provider read from the persisted project schedule settings and the durable calendar registry. Both are passed through `build_project_lifecycle_wsgi_app`.
- **Live evidence (production WSGI + isolated PostgreSQL 14, no repository seeding):** authenticated independent project → working-week calendar, WBS child and one 3-day activity → schedule settings and published snapshot → activity-linked expense `1000` USD → baseline capture reports one activity and `budgeted_cost 1000`, and the raw `p6_baseline_activity` row is `(2026-11-02, 2026-11-04, 1000, USD)` → PATCH progress to Actual Start `2026-11-02`, 40 %, Remaining 2, In Progress → `/reports/catalogue` returns the three subjects and `csv` → definition POST stores revision 1, an identical replay returns revision 1, and the same key with a different body is **409 `REPORT_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_REUSE`** → `?subject=activities&search=Activity` lists only the stored definition, and history is exactly `created` → a run materializes the bound default (`status = In Progress`) into **one row** with `percent_complete 40.0` and `parameter_sources {status: default}`; an explicit `status = Not Started` returns **zero rows** with `explicit` → the runs list holds both ids → a fresh login retrieves the same stored rows and the CSV export answers 200 with `X-Content-SHA256` containing the activity id → a daily schedule is created (revision 1, `next_run_at` set), updated to `10:00` (revision 2), and a stale revision 1 update is **409 `REPORT_SCHEDULE_REVISION_CONFLICT`**; list/read return revision 2 and history is `created`, `updated` → `/p6/earned-value` at Data Date `2026-11-10` measures `budget_at_completion 1000`, `planned_value 1000`, `earned_value 400`, `actual_cost 0` from persisted rows, rejects an unknown client field with **400 `EVM_REQUEST_UNKNOWN_FIELD`**, and honours a later Data Date → viewer lists reports, reads the stored run and measures EVM **200** but creating a definition is **403 `REPORT_PERMISSION_DENIED`** and a schedule is **403 `REPORT_SCHEDULE_PERMISSION_DENIED`** → another tenant gets **403** on reports and EVM → `project_report_definition` holds `(REP-1, 1)`, `project_report_run` holds exactly two runs generated by the planner, `project_report_schedule` holds `(SCH-1, daily, 1)`, and the baseline rows are byte-for-byte unchanged after every measurement and export.
- **Tests:** full Python suite with isolated PostgreSQL 14 — **5,590 passed / 20 skipped / 15 subtests**; operationalization live module — **24 passed** (including the new reports/EVM test); migration probe on a throw-away schema — ledger 21, applied 21, **107 tables**, drift `None`, and an immediate re-apply reports `already_applied` 1–21 with the same fingerprint. Web unchanged.
- **Not changed:** no auth/RLS weakening, no second report engine or second earned-value calculation, no production or Supabase write, and no Showcase record (CUBI-REF-001/002/003, CUBI-DAM-001) read, written or deleted. Migration 21 was applied only to throw-away local schemas.
- **Honest limits:** the live proof runs on an isolated schema, not the owner production database; the report dataset reads activities, resources and risks only (no cost or budget subject) and the schedule runner is not driven by a daemon — schedules are stored, claimable and auditable through the API, but a deployment still needs an external caller for due occurrences; `enterprise_dashboards*` and `p6_report_profile_*` remain unwired and belong to a dashboard/profile slice; EVM assignment decomposition stays disabled because a baseline activity budget is not a priced assignment split; the baseline is revision-scoped, so a later project revision requires a fresh capture before measurement (the API answers 409/empty rather than reinterpreting old rows).
- **Status:** PR open; merge only after every CI job is green. Next dependency in the same mission: Project Closeout/Reopening, then the Integrated Engineering Acceptance.

## 2026-11-25 — P0 Engineering Phase E (twelfth slice): Project Closeout and Reopening — Issue #2138

- **main SHA at branch start:** `2d08f0d85ff38241bf8882ddad81c1da62944da4` (verified against `origin/main`; the #2160 merge). **Correction carried forward:** slice 11 recorded PR #2160 as open; it is **MERGED** as `2d08f0d85ff38241bf8882ddad81c1da62944da4`.
- **Branch / head:** `feat/engineering-project-closeout`, feature commit `6f34ca2e`. PR to be opened from this branch.
- **Audit result:** no closeout contract existed anywhere in the product. `WEB_SURFACE_TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md` GAP-PRJ-02 recorded "no close/archive operation beyond the status field"; punch items are not durable in the production runtime, and the V26 project status is a plain Active/Inactive/Planned/What-If value, so overloading it would have made a status edit silently reopen a finished project. The only durable, gateable fact was the P6 issue register (`ISSUE_OPEN_STATUSES = {open, in_progress}`, previously declared but unused), so closeout was designed as its own append-only register rather than a second lifecycle model.
- **Change (new bounded domain, no scheduling/cost recalculation):** `project_closeout.py` adds `PostgresProjectCloseoutStore` (`project_closeout` current row + `project_closeout_event` append-only log), `ProjectCloseoutService` and `ProjectCloseoutAPI` under contract `project-closeout.v1`. Close and reopen each open one transaction; a same-state request is an idempotent no-op even with a stale revision; a real change is a compare-and-set on the record's own revision (`INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` for revision 1, conditional `UPDATE … WHERE record_revision = expected` afterwards) and a lost race raises `CLOSEOUT_REVISION_CONFLICT` → 409. Closing is refused while any issue is `open`/`in_progress` with `CLOSEOUT_OPEN_ISSUES:{n}`; reopening records the current open issue count. Reads require `PROJECT_READ`, writes `PROJECT_ADMIN` plus actor/session equality. The route branch exposes `GET /api/projects/{id}/closeout`, `GET .../closeout/history`, `POST .../closeout/close` and `POST .../closeout/reopen`; the request body is `{expected_revision:int, note:str}`, malformed bodies answer 400 `CLOSEOUT_REQUEST_INVALID`, and the WSGI app now carries the query string for `/closeout`.
- **Migration 22 `project-closeout`:** creates both tables and extends the existing REVOKE + restrictive-RLS hardening (`cubi_block_direct_api_clients`) to `project_closeout` and `project_closeout_event`. Probe on a throw-away schema: ledger 22, applied 22, **109 tables**, drift `None`; re-apply reports `already_applied` 1–22 with the same fingerprint.
- **Live evidence (production WSGI + isolated PostgreSQL 14):** independent project created for the test with its own memberships → `GET /closeout` reads `open` / revision 0 / no timestamps → `GET /closeout/history` is `[]` → a viewer reads 200, a planner `POST close` is **403 `CLOSEOUT_PERMISSION_DENIED`**, another tenant is **403** → an open issue makes admin close **409 `CLOSEOUT_OPEN_ISSUES:1`** and `project_closeout` stays empty → resolving the issue lets close succeed: state `closed`, revision 1, `closed_by` the admin user, `closed_at` set → a repeated close with a stale revision is **200** and still revision 1 with exactly **one** event → reopen at revision 1 returns `open` revision 2 with `closed_at`/`closed_by` cleared; a repeated reopen is **200** revision 2 → a second open issue blocks re-close, and after closing it the close succeeds at revision 3 → a reopen with stale revision 1 is **409 `CLOSEOUT_REVISION_CONFLICT`** and the stored row stays `("closed", 3)` → missing/bool `expected_revision` and an unknown subpath answer **400 `CLOSEOUT_REQUEST_INVALID`** → final reopen at revision 3 yields revision 4; history is exactly `(1 closed, 2 reopened, 3 closed, 4 reopened)` with the admin actor and the four notes in order → `project_closeout` is `("open", 4, "", "final reopen", 0)`, `project_closeout_event` holds `closed-1`, `reopened-2`, `closed-3`, `reopened-4`, and `project_lifecycle_projects` is still `("active", 0)`, proving closeout is not an alias of status → cross-tenant history stays **403**.
- **Unit evidence:** `tests/test_project_closeout.py` (11 tests) proves the default-open read and scope checks, the open-issue gate with no write, revision/event shape, idempotent close and reopen, stale-revision conflict, PROJECT_ADMIN plus actor equality, note trimming to 2,000 characters, rejection of a naive timestamp, non-empty error DTO messages for every category, and the API's bounded JSON contract.
- **Tests:** full Python suite with isolated PostgreSQL 14 — **5,602 passed / 20 skipped / 15 subtests**; operationalization live module — **25 passed** (including the new closeout test); project-lifecycle routes/wsgi, production web app, migration parity, postgres hardening, production readiness and vercel entrypoints — **212 passed**; closeout unit module — **11 passed**. Web unchanged.
- **Not changed:** no auth/RLS weakening, no second scheduling, cost or issue model, no production or Supabase write, and no Showcase record (CUBI-REF-001/002/003, CUBI-DAM-001) read, written or deleted. Migration 22 was applied only to throw-away local schemas.
- **Honest limits:** the live proof runs on an isolated schema, not the owner production database; closing is gated only on the P6 issue register because punch items, checklists and document approvals are not durable production facts yet, so a later punch-list register must extend the gate rather than bypass it; closeout is decoupled from the workspace/settings revision by design, so a close does not freeze schedule or budget writes — an "closed projects are read-only" policy is a separate decision; there is no archive/delete operation and no Web surface consumes the contract yet (server contract only).
- **Status:** PR open; merge only after every CI job is green. Next dependency in the same mission: the Integrated Engineering Acceptance.

A third defect: the `Filters` context command focused the quick-search box instead of opening the Filters dialog. V26 Help documents shortcut menus (HTML `use_shortcut_menus.htm`, Help 815 physical p. 38) and resource assignment through Activity Details → Resources → Add Resource (HTML `assign_resources_to_activities.htm`, p. 219); it documents Excel handoff as Range Copy/Paste (p. 208, 370, 745), **not** an Export to Excel command — so the one-click workbook is a documented CUBI extension, not a vendor parity claim.

Activity delete remains disabled with a true reason because the core has no delete repository or route.
- **Server contract proof:** `tests/test_exchange_api.py::test_activities_workbook_export_carries_a_verifiable_digest` proves 200, `spreadsheetml.sheet`, `activities.xlsx`, header digest equal to the SHA-256 of the body, and 401/403 without a session.

Scenario 02 downloads a real `activities.xlsx` of 6,016 bytes whose SHA-256 equals both the server header and the hash computed from the response body; scenario 03 activates the Assignments window with `activity-id = A110` and stored assignments stay 0 → 0 (no invented record). Evidence copied to `docs/workspace/evidence/activity-export-assign-v26-2026-11-25/` (JSON + 3 screenshots).
- **Unit evidence:** `apps/web/src/workspace-activity-export.test.ts` (7 tests: path contract, filename parsing including `filename*=UTF-8''`, path traversal, digest match/mismatch, empty body, transport without download, server error passthrough); updated `workspace-view.test.ts` asserts both menu items are present and enabled without a reason.
- **Tests:** full Python suite (DSN unset) — **5,295 passed / 270 skipped / 15 subtests / 0 failed**; web `npm test` — **1,359 / 1,359** and **16 / 16**; `tsc` and `esbuild` build exit 0; the new e2e 4/4 PASS against the local preview runtime on port 4207.
- **Docs:** `WEB_SURFACE_TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md` gains schedule rows 14–15 and GAP-V26-02 (resolved); `ACTIVITIES_V26_COMPLIANCE_MATRIX_2026-11-08.md` marks Assign/Export resolved, records the Filters wiring fix, and adds a v26 crosswalk with HTML URLs and Help 815 physical page numbers.
- **Not changed:** no scheduling engine, no second workbook builder, no API/storage contract change, no Production or Supabase write, no Showcase record read or written, and no Oracle/Primavera/P6 branding in the product UI. Preview ≠ Production.
- **Honest limits:** the exported workbook is a CUBI extension (P6 documents copy/paste); the row-menu Assign routes to CUBI's Assignments window while P6 documents the Details → Resources path (both write through the same service); activity delete has no core capability and stays disabled; numeric parity against a licensed V26 run remains unproven; the Preview deployment has not been re-verified from this sandbox.

## 2026-11-25 — P0 Engineering Phase E (Activities window slice 14): persisted calendar exceptions on the Gantt non-working bands — Issue #2138

- **main SHA at branch start:** `768e6f6ce89623bbfef9663eb9f93249d1ea0661` (verified against `origin/main`; the #2162 merge). **Correction carried forward:** slice 13 recorded PR #2162 as open; it is **MERGED** as `768e6f6ce89623bbfef9663eb9f93249d1ea0661`.
- **Branch / head:** `feat/engineering-gantt-calendar-exceptions`, feature commit `b218e5a76`. PR **#2163** to be merged only after every CI job is green.
- **Audit result:** the Activities Gantt shaded weekends and stored holidays from the version-bound `calendar_snapshot`, but persisted `calendar_exception` rows lived in a separate mutable store (and could be inherited from a base calendar), so nonwork/holiday/working-hour exceptions never reached the bands. The compliance matrix recorded this explicitly as a limitation. Classifying those dates in TypeScript would have duplicated Shared Core resolution (four exception modes, inheritance precedence, weekly work-hour rules), so the fix is a server projection.
- **Change (no second calendar engine, no duplicated logic):** new `src/construction_pm/p6_calendar_day_facts_api.py` exposes `P6CalendarDayFactsAPI` under contract **`p6-calendar-day-facts.v1`**. It validates scope/permission, walks the calendar inheritance chain with cycle and incomplete-reference detection, loads each version's snapshot and work-hour rules, applies `apply_weekly_work_hour_rules`, translates persisted exceptions through `persisted_exception_to_kernel`, and builds the same `CalendarResolverRegistry` used by scheduling. It returns only the exception dates with `{date, working, source}` — no range query and no browser arithmetic. `GET /api/projects/{project_id}/p6/calendars/{calendar_id}/{calendar_version}/day-facts` is wired in both `preview/runtime.py` and `deployment/production_runtime.py` through `production_composition.py`; 404 covers missing calendar/snapshot/base, 409 covers inheritance cycles, and unsupported modes fail closed with `P6_CALENDAR_DAY_FACTS_UNRESOLVED` rather than being guessed away.
- **Web behaviour:** `loadCalendarDayFacts` (`workspace-calendars.ts`) validates the contract, identity and every override; `workspace-read-api.ts` projects overrides into `workingDates`/`nonWorkingDates`; `createGanttNonWorkingBands` applies precedence — a working override removes a weekend/holiday band, a nonwork or stored-holiday exception adds a holiday band, and a failed projection keeps the snapshot bands while shading no exception date (`dayFactsErrorCode`).
- **Unit/API evidence:** `tests/test_p6_calendar_day_facts_api.py` — 11 passed (no exceptions, nonwork, reset-standard preserving the stored holiday definition, total/detailed work hours on Saturday, inherited source, local-over-inherited, working-time calendar, unknown calendar, read permission, cross-scope). `tests/test_project_lifecycle_http_routes.py` proves the 200 payload, unknown-calendar 404 and unauthenticated 401. Web coverage in `workspace-gantt.test.ts` (three band-precedence cases), `workspace-read-api.test.ts` (override projection and failed projection) and `workspace-view.test.ts` (persisted exception overrides over snapshot shading).
- **Browser evidence (local full-stack preview, SQLite, real WSGI, real Chrome):** `apps/web/e2e/gantt-calendar-exceptions-v26.mjs`, contract **`cubi-gantt-calendar-exceptions-v26.v1`**, **5/5 scenarios PASS** — baseline bands, `POST` nonwork on 2026-11-05 adds a band and day-facts reports `working:false`, total-work-hours 4 on Saturday 2026-10-31 removes that weekend band while Sunday stays, `DELETE` restores the baseline, and the run ends with zero exceptions/console errors/5xx. Evidence copied to `docs/workspace/evidence/gantt-calendar-exceptions-v26-2026-11-25/` (JSON + 4 screenshots). `node --check` on the e2e script and `py_compile` on both new Python files pass.
- **Tests:** full Python suite (DSN unset) — **5,307 passed / 270 skipped / 0 failed / 15 subtests**; web `npm test` — **1,365 / 1,365** and preview-server **16 / 16**.
- **Docs:** `ACTIVITIES_V26_COMPLIANCE_MATRIX_2026-11-08.md` row 40 now records the day-facts path and keeps only the missing visibility toggle as a gap, plus a new dated slice section; `ENGINEERING_GANTT_RELATIONSHIPS_CALENDARS.md` updates `ENG-WS-GANTT` shading, `ENG-CAL-06`, `ENG-GANTT-16` scenario, dependencies, error scenarios and divergence row 5; `WEB_SURFACE_TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md` records the endpoint and exception-aware bands on the schedule and calendars rows.
- **Not changed:** no scheduling engine, no second calendar model, no API/storage contract break, no Production or Supabase write, no Showcase record read or written, and no Oracle/Primavera/P6 branding in the product UI. Preview ≠ Production.
- **Honest limits:** the Bars dialog still has no Calendar non-work-time visibility toggle; day-facts lists exception dates only (it is not a range query, by design); the preview runtime remains an ephemeral SQLite fixture and was not re-verified against the Vercel deployment from this sandbox; numeric parity against a licensed V26 run remains unproven.
- **Status:** PR #2163 open; merge only after every CI job is green.

- **What was added:** `tests/integration/test_engineering_acceptance_postgres_live.py` — one module-scoped throw-away schema, one set of real identities (admin, planner, viewer in tenant A; an outsider in tenant B), and an eleven-stage acceptance run.   5. Progress and Data Date: PATCH the predecessor to Actual Start `2026-11-10`, 60 %, Remaining 2, In Progress → republish → Tracking `finish_variance_days` equals `(finish − planned_finish)` and is **positive**; clearing the Data Date **shrinks** the variance and restoring it reproduces the identical value; Workspace cells keep `activity.planned_duration` `4`/`3`; masters keep `duration_value` and `planned_finish`; the raw `p6_baseline_activity` rows are byte-for-byte unchanged.

Earned value: the default ETC method **fails closed** with **400 `EARNED_VALUE_REMAINING_COST_REQUIRED`** because no per-activity remaining cost is stored; with `etc_method=PF_ONE` the same baseline measures **BAC 1000 / PV 1000 / EV 600 / AC 0**, i.e. the measurement reads the captured baseline, not the 1450 budget register.

  11. Final consistency read: the rescheduling result hash is unchanged, `scheduling_jobs` is `("SUCCEEDED", 1)`, the baseline rows are identical to capture, `activity_master` still stores durations `4`/`3` with the original planned finishes, the budget line is revision 2 at `1200.00`, `project_closeout` is `("open", 2)` with events `closed→reopened`, and `project_lifecycle_projects.status` is still `active`.

- **Not changed:** no product code, no migration, no schema change, no auth/RLS weakening, no production or Supabase write, and no Showcase record (CUBI-REF-001/002/003, CUBI-DAM-001) read, written or deleted.

## 2026-11-26 — Remaining-evidence audit: row verification, Preview root cause, V26 parity — Issue #2138

**Scope:** read-only audit of the three remaining acceptance items, on main `58ff62f98611f2ef4d7eb92066e779092646a3e7` (PR #2165 merged). No product code, migration, schema, Auth/RLS, permission, environment variable, domain, deployment or Production/Supabase/Showcase data was changed. Re-verified on the merge commit: `postgres`, `postgres-sync`, `test (3.11)`, `test (3.12)`, `test (3.13)` and all four `typecheck` jobs are **success**.

**Access audit (performed first, as instructed).** `env` contains no Supabase/P6/Vercel/DSN credential of any kind; `/mnt/session/uploads` is empty and no file is mounted; no Vercel or database token exists. Anonymous probes are therefore the only production observation available.

### Item 1 — Row-level verification of CUBI-REF-001/002/003, CUBI-DAM-001 and the four Showcase projects — **BLOCKED**

- **Evidence of the block:** on both live application origins, `GET /api/projects` and `GET /api/shared-projects` return **401 `SESSION_REQUIRED`**; the short domain has no API at all (404). There is no disposable identity, no read-only DSN, and no authorization to create either. Row contents cannot be read anonymously, and the readiness payload proves only schema version/table count, not rows.
- **What is already proven without row access:** the live database is reachable and migrated — the current main deployment reports schema **22 / 109 tables** with structural drift empty (only the known step-5 registry checksum, `fd930754ba53` vs `31e9fec9fa71`). It is therefore not a schema blocker.
- **Exact unblock required:** one of (a) a one-time read-only PostgreSQL DSN (role limited to `SELECT` on `public`, no DDL/DML) for `CUBI-Platform-Core-DB` ref `pbgosszuzemljgoccppk`, or (b) a disposable authenticated login on the canonical origin plus explicit authorization to issue read-only GETs. Either is sufficient; no write is requested.
- **Planned read-only query set (not executed):** project presence in `project_lifecycle_projects` and `cubi_reference_project` for the four IDs; per-project counts from `activity_master`, `wbs_master`, `relationship_master`, `p6_resource_master`, `p6_resource_assignment`, `p6_baseline`, `p6_baseline_activity`, `calendar_master`, `p6_expense`, `p6_issue`, `project_budget_line`; the `cubi_reference_import` journal row and `counts_json`; then compare against the recorded source contract: REF-001/002 = 180 activities, 238 relationships, 120 WBS, 30 resources, 240 assignments, 1 baseline / 180 baseline rows, 8 budgets, 8 expenses, 8 issues; REF-003 = 180 / 238 / 135 with the same enterprise counts; CUBI-DAM-001 generator = 720 activities, 192 WBS, 730 relationships, 120 resources, 1,440 assignments, 720 baseline rows. A count mismatch or a missing row is a finding, not an adjustment.
- **Status:** **BLOCKED** — no fabricated rows, no writes, no inferred "present".

### Item 2 — Canonical Preview: root cause, deployment, domain, routing and health — **PASS (diagnosis only; no setting changed)**

- **Evidence artifact:** `docs/workspace/evidence/preview-alias-root-cause-2026-11-26/probe-results.json` (anonymous curl probes, captured 2026-11-26 ~02:28–02:36 UTC).
- **Short domain `https://cubi-web-preview.vercel.app` — this is the "unhealthy" origin:** `/` returns 200 with the **static marketing landing page** (3,847 bytes, `landing-entry.bundle.js`, canonical self); `/login` **404**, `/app` 200 static shell (1,723 bytes), `/api/health/live` **404**, `/api/health/ready` **404**, `/api/projects` **404**, `/api/shared-projects` **404**. It is a domain assigned to an older static public-site deployment, not to the Python application. Root cause: domain/deployment assignment, not routing or code.
- **Production domain `https://cubi-web-preview-foroughichamshir-7811.vercel.app`:** serves deployment `cubi-web-preview-2y8d6xaco-…`; `/login` 200 (22,076 bytes, "CUBI Workspace — Sign in"), `/api/health/live` 200 `{"status":"ok"}`, `/api/health/ready` 200 with database healthy but schema **13 / 59 tables**, `/api/projects` 401 `SESSION_REQUIRED`. The application is alive; it is an **old build** (the pre-migration-14 registry) and does not match main.
- **Current main deployment `https://cubi-web-preview-5dfo52ssm-…` (also `…-git-main-…`):** `/login` 200, `/api/health/ready` 200 with schema **22 / 109 tables**, structural drift `[]`, only the known step-5 checksum pair. So main code is deployed, the database is migrated, and the missing piece is promotion/alias assignment.
- **Root cause (two layers, no code defect):** (1) the short domain is pinned to a static marketing deployment; (2) the project's production domain is pinned to deployment `2y8d6xaco` (13/59) instead of the current `5dfo52ssm` (22/109). Neither is a health-service, database or routing failure.
- **Remediation documented, not executed (dashboard only):** promote `5dfo52ssm` (or a newer main build) to Production so the production domain follows main; reassign `cubi-web-preview.vercel.app` to that deployment or declare one canonical origin; set `CONSTRUCTION_PM_PUBLIC_ORIGIN` to exactly that origin; verify `/login` 200, `/api/health/ready` 200 at 22/109 with structural drift empty, `/app` 302 `/login` without a session, `/api/projects` 401. No Vercel token exists here, so no deployment was promoted, assigned or redeployed.
- **Status:** **PASS** for the mandated root-cause/diagnosis deliverable; the alias itself remains unrepaired by design.

### Item 3 — Numeric parity against a licensed P6 V26 run — **BLOCKED**

- **Evidence:** no licensed P6 V26 installation, database, export (`XER`, Primavera XML, XLSX) or calculated reference output exists in the repository, `/mnt` or the sandbox. The only V26 artifact is the public *P6 Professional 26 User Guide* PDF in `/tmp`, which is a help document, not a licensed run. `GAP-V26-01` in `WEB_SURFACE_TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md` and `CORE_COMPLETION_EXECUTION_LEDGER.md` already records this; no row is `IMPLEMENTED_AND_PARITY_TESTED`.
- **Input-equivalence checklist a licensed run must satisfy:** identical project scope and activity IDs; activity types and percent-complete types; durations and duration units; project, activity and resource calendars with the same workweeks, shifts and exceptions; the same WBS, relationships (type and lag with units) and constraints; the same Data Date and must-finish-by; identical schedule options (float, open ends, out-of-sequence, leveling, actual-date rules); identical resource master, units, rates, availability and assignments (units/time, remaining/actual, driving flag, curves); identical budget, cost accounts, currency and expense facts.
- **Comparison matrix to be filled with numbers:** per-activity ES/EF/LS/LF, total/free float, critical flag, project finish; cost (budgeted/actual/remaining and cost-account rollups); progress (percent complete, performance percent, units); EVM at the same Data Date and ETC method (PV/EV/AC/BAC/CV/SV/CPI/SPI/ETC/EAC/VAC); reports (activity detail, WBS summary, resource histogram, cash-flow/S-curve). Tolerances must be declared before the run: exact dates, money at 2 decimals, indices at 4 decimals. Every difference gets a technical cause (calendar resolution, float convention, lag semantics, ETC method, cost distribution, leveling) and either a documented divergence or a bounded defect PR.
- **Status:** **BLOCKED** — no licensed run; parity is not claimed.

**Closing statement for this audit:** Item 1 **BLOCKED** (no disposable identity / read-only DSN), Item 2 **PASS** for root-cause and remediation evidence (alias not modified), Item 3 **BLOCKED** (no licensed V26 run). No Production/Supabase/Showcase write, no Auth/RLS or permission change, and no isolated test presented as environment evidence.

## 2026-11-26 — P0 Engineering Phase E (Activities window slice 15): Bars-dialog calendar non-working visibility switch — Issue #2138

- **main SHA at branch start:** `74cd469824df5fad0e0eb754c68fe007de0897ac` (verified against `origin/main`; the #2169 merge). The branch was first created from a stale local `main` (`272ca6c5`) and **rebased onto `74cd46982`** before this head, so the PR base is the current main. **Correction carried forward:** slice 14 recorded PR #2163 as open; it is **MERGED** as `272ca6c5a4bb7d44707e977929b6526abca1fb08`.
- **Branch / head:** `feat/engineering-bars-calendar-nonworking-toggle`, feature commit `dad9948d0a8492049e123da88c648fd4c5cff08e` (rebased); the docs/evidence commit follows it on the same branch and is the PR head. PR **#2171**.
- **Audit result:** the Activities Bars dialog (`ganttChartEditor()` in `workspace-dialogs.ts`) exposed bar labels, legend, sight lines, Data Date line and progress line, but no control over the calendar non-working band layer; the compliance matrix recorded it as the only remaining gap after slice 14. P6 Professional v26 HTML Help, topic **"Change a Gantt Chart bar's settings"** (`change_a_gantt_chart_bar_s_settings.htm`, Customizing Activity Layouts > Gantt Chart), documents `Calendar nonwork time` **only as a per-bar Bar Settings necking option**; it documents no global band-visibility control. The new band switch is therefore recorded as a CUBI-specific display extension, and the per-bar necking option (`ENG-GANTT-09`) remains separate and unchanged.
- **Change (display-only, no second preference store, no reload):** `GanttChartSettings.showCalendarNonWorkingTime` defaults to `true`; `normalizeGanttSettings` falls back to the default so legacy layouts without the key stay shaded. The shared Gantt chart options in the Bars dialog add a checked-by-default **Show calendar non-working time** checkbox with an explicit note distinguishing it from the per-bar necking setting. `readLayoutPatch` reads it and `applyLayoutOptions` persists it through the existing `rememberVisualPreferences` + `commitLayout` path into the saved layout `metadata.presentation` (revision bump on layout only). `workspace-view.ts` gates only `createGanttNonWorkingBands`/the `.cp-gantt-nonworking` layer; bars, baseline/progress layers, relationship lines, WBS bands, rows, axis, calendar snapshot, day-facts projection and the project schedule revision are untouched, and applying the switch re-renders without a page/project reload.
- **Unit evidence:** `workspace-presentation.test.ts` proves default `true`, fallback for `{}`/`gantt:{}` and rejection of non-boolean junk; `workspace-dialogs.test.ts` proves the checked markup, parsing, and independence from per-bar `bars.*` state; `workspace-view.test.ts` proves that `showCalendarNonWorkingTime: false` removes every `[data-gantt-nonworking]` band while rows, critical bars, links and the axis remain.
- **Browser evidence (local full-stack preview, SQLite, real WSGI, real Chrome; official Preview unavailable):** `apps/web/e2e/gantt-calendar-nonworking-toggle-v26.mjs`, contract **`cubi-gantt-calendar-nonworking-toggle-v26.v1`**, **14/14 scenario records PASS**, 37 screenshots in en/LTR and fa/RTL at 1280×800, 1440×900 and 1920×1080. The switch is checked by default; hiding it removes all 11 bands (10 weekend + 1 holiday) while bar geometry (17 bars), 15 relationship links, WBS bands, rows and axis are byte-identical; showing it restores the exact 11-band list; the value survives a reload after `localStorage.clear()` (so it is restored from the saved layout, not browser-local prefs); project revision and the calendar day-facts projection are unchanged; zero runtime exceptions, console errors or HTTP 5xx. Evidence: `docs/workspace/evidence/gantt-calendar-nonworking-toggle-v26-2026-11-26/` (report JSON + 37 PNG).
- **Tests (measured on the rebased branch at `74cd46982`):** full Python suite (DSN unset) — **5,311 passed / 272 skipped / 15 subtests / 0 failed**; web `npm test` — **1,369 / 1,369** and preview-server **16 / 16**; `tsc --noEmit` exit 0; `npm run build` (tsc build + esbuild) exit 0; `node --check` on the new e2e script passes; the E2E was re-run against the rebuilt rebased bundle and stayed 14/14 PASS.
- **Open PR ownership check:** #2048 (26 commits), #1716 (95 commits) and #1698 (100 commits) are all `CONFLICTING`/`DIRTY` against current `main`. All three touch `apps/web/public/styles.css` (and `apps/web/styles.css`); #2048 and #1716 also touch `apps/web/src/main.ts`, `workspace-desktop-shell.ts` and `workspace-view.ts`; #2048 additionally touches `workspace-toolbar-registry.ts`. None touches `workspace-dialogs.ts` or `workspace-presentation.ts`. This slice only inserts an additive, gated condition in `workspace-view.ts` and new fields/markup in files those PRs do not own; no branch was rebased, rewritten or deleted.
- **Docs:** `ACTIVITIES_V26_COMPLIANCE_MATRIX_2026-11-08.md` row 40 is now `مطابق و تأییدشده` with a dated slice section recording the Help authority and the CUBI-specific divergence; `ENGINEERING_GANTT_RELATIONSHIPS_CALENDARS.md` updates the `ENG-WS-GANTT` shading row, the Gantt dialogs list, `ENG-GANTT-16` scenario, divergence row 5 and the executed-acceptance row; `WEB_SURFACE_TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md` updates the schedule and calendars rows and adds resolved gap **GAP-V26-03**.
- **Not changed:** no scheduling/CPM engine, no `CalendarResolverRegistry` or work-hours rule change, no new calendar engine, no snapshot or day-facts contract change, no parallel preference store, no separate Bars dialog, no API/storage change, no Production or Supabase write, no Showcase record read or written, and no Oracle/Primavera/P6 branding in the product UI. Preview ≠ Production.
- **Honest limits:** the remaining-evidence audit on main (2026-11-26) recorded the Preview alias/domain root cause, but the **exact mandated alias** (`https://cubi-web-preview-git-feat-vercel-p-8eb184-foroughichamshir-7811.vercel.app`) still answered HTTP 404 `DEPLOYMENT_NOT_FOUND` at test time, so no official-Preview behavioural claim is made and all browser evidence comes from the local full-stack preview; P6 v26 Help has no global band-visibility control, so the switch is a CUBI display extension while the per-bar `Calendar nonwork time` necking option remains the vendor-documented behaviour; numeric parity against a licensed V26 run remains unproven.

- **Audit result:** the runtime already implemented `filterAndSortActivities`/`buildWorkspaceDisplayRows` with `groupLevels`/`sortLevels`, `MAX_GROUP_LEVELS = 3`, and the Group and Sort dialog already rendered three group and three sort rows. The compliance matrix still recorded only the first level as browser-tested, and the visual pass exposed a real defect: `.cp-group-row` inherited the six-column `.cp-filter-row` grid, so each group field `<select>` collapsed to **26 px** and the selected field (WBS / Activity Status / Critical) was invisible in both en and fa.
- **Change:** no scheduling or grouping-engine rewrite. Added the matching `.cp-group-row` rules (two-column grid, label and select sizing) to `apps/web/styles.css` (and the served `apps/web/public/styles.css` copy). Extended `apps/web/e2e/activity-group-sort-v26.mjs` with scenarios 10–13 and added two unit cases: three-level nesting with per-level directions in `workspace-wbs-display.test.ts`, and grouped fields removed from the in-band sort plan in `workspace-filter-sort.test.ts`.

- **Verified behaviour:** three levels nest as WBS (asc) > Activity Status (desc) > Critical (asc) with band counts derived from stored facts; three sort levels (Total Float asc, Activity Name desc, Early Finish asc) order the innermost band; WBS bands follow `sequenceNumber` then `localCode` and stay identical after a refresh; the whole three-level group plan and three-level sort plan persist in `metadata.groupSort` and restore after reload; table and Gantt row order agree; Persian RTL at 1440×900 has localized field labels, no horizontal overflow, and all three levels visible.

The report records `testedCodeSha`/`gitHead` `49255018c`. Evidence: `docs/workspace/evidence/activity-group-sort-v26-2026-11-26/`.
- **Open PR ownership check:** #2048, #1716 and #1698 remain `CONFLICTING`/`DIRTY`; #2048 and #1716 touch `workspace-view.ts`, and all three touch `apps/web/styles.css` / `apps/web/public/styles.css`. None touches `workspace-filter-sort.ts`, `workspace-grouping.ts`, `workspace-wbs-display.ts`, `workspace-dialogs.ts` or `activity-group-sort-v26.mjs`. Our `styles.css` change is a three-selector additive rule for `.cp-group-row` that does not alter any existing selector.
- **Docs:** the compliance matrix row 33 is now `مطابق و تأییدشده` with a dated acceptance section and updated limitation; `ENGINEERING_WORKSPACE_ACTIVITIES.md` updates `ENG-WS-GROUPING` (group and sort rows) and the `ENG-WS-ACT-05` scenario; `WEB_SURFACE_TRACEABILITY_MATRIX.md` updates the schedule row and adds resolved **GAP-V26-04**.
- **Not changed:** no CPM/calendar/working-time calculation, no snapshot or day-facts contract, no group/sort data model change, no new preference store, no API/storage change, no Production or Supabase write, no Oracle/Primavera/P6 branding.
